# The roadmap's history, moved out of it on 2026-10-16

When v0.4.0's part 1 was done, the roadmap was trimmed to what's ahead: Next, Alongside, the decisions, Directions, Later and what's taken when touched, Maintenance, and Deferred. What it held of finished work is here verbatim, so a link into it still finds its words: v0.3.1's and part 1's items, Done, the smaller items, the entries taken when touched that are done or dropped, Done by the run that asked, and the triage of each review.Its links are rewritten for this directory, and #anchors that pointed into the roadmap point into the roadmap still.

## From Next: v0.3.1, and v0.4.0 part 1, whole

**Now: v0.3.1.** v0.3.0 was tagged 2026-10-15 on rc11's commit, 285a496f, after the full stage passed under the bar (the project's plan/full-stage-final-rc11.md), and published as a GitHub Release; macports/macports-ports#34756, the port, was updated to it through dockhand and merged the same day. v0.4.0's part 1 started at the person's word (2026-10-15). SQL-S1 is on main (3ec7b0ea). v0.3.1 waits on the quick stage, `dockhand clean` on the M1's v0.3.0 database, and the person's word for the tag.

Done on main since rc11, for v0.4.0: `submit --check` runs the check an earlier one left unfinished ([note](2026-10-13-submit-check-gap.md)), a kept `--note` is said as kept and flagged where it repeats the commit body ([note](2026-10-13-kept-note.md)), `--with-obsolete` no longer fails on an obsolete stub the edit moved already ([note](2026-10-14-obsolete-stub-moved.md)), and `submit --rewrite-description` takes over a description dockhand didn't write, at the person's word for #34756 ([note](2026-10-15-rewrite-description.md)). A locked login Keychain said plainly went into rc11 ([note](2026-10-10-locked-keychain.md)); X2, L2b, L3a, L3e, L5, A-H8, and M3's test into rc7 (batches 110 and 111).

1. **v0.3.1: SQL-S1, the quadratic `PruneHistory`, as a point release on the quick stage** (the SQL study, [review](../reviews/2026-10-13-sql-study.md); the first batch after v0.3.0, ahead of part 1's checksums). `PruneHistory` (store/sqlite/records.go) and `Cleanup` (engine/clean.go), with `cleanup.after` at 15 days, grow quadratically with history: past 5 s the prune fails other writers, a running check's result among them, while serve runs its checks, and past 30 s it rolls back every day, so archive and journal pruning never run again; the 5 s point comes at roughly an eighth of a year's history, by the study's step timings (an inference; no real database was measured). v0.3.0 is what the MacPorts port ships through v0.4.0's cycle, and every real database starts pruning in it. One batch: migration 031, the `run_plan`, `run_revision`, `plan_revision`, and `result_reused` indexes, and `DROP INDEX result_archive` (SQL-S3's dead index); the executions OR split into two NOT EXISTS; the results keep-set as a MATERIALIZED CTE, which part 1's rule for a dependency's newest kept archive extends; SQL-S2's chunked prune, built as one store-internal delete in foreign-key order over branch IDs, which part 3's `clean --delete` reuses for one branch; and plan tests rejecting a correlated SCAN in every prune and window statement. docs/development.md gains two rules with it: every foreign-key child column of a pruned table has an index, and no write transaction exceeds about a second at year scale. v0.3.1 is cut from 3892f092, main's last commit before part 1's first batch (its last code commit is af476dc0), on release/v0.3, which carries the setup fix for a guest MacPorts newer than asked, 147dc4a9 (07fae561 on main); v0.3.1-rc1 is 147dc4a9, and everything else after 3892f092 goes into v0.4.0. It was to be main right after the batch: main past rc11 holds only small fixes before it (b1ac0ce2 and 4ea079d7, with 304e8d42's test), and 031 stays 031, so later schema work starts at 032. Its QA is the quick stage, and, after upgrading, `dockhand clean` on the M1's own v0.3.0 database, which the full pass and soak left: a real 030 to 031 migration and a real prune. The tag waits on the person's word. Kept out, to keep it small: the `prune.go` and `cleanup.go` moves, and X5, in part 1's hygiene.
2. **v0.4.0, part 1: checksums and the probe, typed holds, and the fixes** (the coalesced roadmap's v0.3.1; about 8 batches, one of them the split's small relocations and `term`). Each is isolated, or built in the shape later work keeps. From here on, an item adds no new package-level test seam; the host value collects them in part 3 (the command package review, [review](../reviews/2026-10-15-command-package-review.md), coalesced the same day at the person's "Send it", 07:13Z).
   - **a. Checksums and the version probe, built as layer 1's step 2**, one batch, first in the release, as the item with a monthly cost. "Checksums on just about anything" means no version probe, none of update's holds, and `--plan` as the verify mode; `go_toolchain.setup` and any unknown `<pg>.setup` offer their literal words to the existing probe as candidates rather than growing `setupVersionIndex`; and what `checksums` couldn't do is a typed list, not prose, which step 2 later shortens. qmake5's host-state split moves to layer 1's step 3, and `checksums` stops refusing cpi without it. The two entries it builds:
     - **First: the Go toolchain ports update, and their checksums refresh** (field testing at 1cc87c43, 2026-10-08, first within v0.3.1 as the one item with a monthly cost on ports much used, and kept out of the release candidates, which refuse it clearly; PRs #35374 and #35375 written by hand). `update --outdated go-1.26 go-1.27` skipped both, "no editable version input established", though outdated found them behind: their version is `go_toolchain.setup 1.26.8` (PortGroup go_toolchain 1.0), with three distfiles, the source and two darwin binaries, and `checksums go-1.26` after a hand edit said to write them yourself. Batch 80 taught update garage's `gitea.setup`. Gated by the probe, not by a table of positions (the prioritization thread, 2026-10-08): `setupVersionIndex` (macports/portfile/version.go) stays the fast path for the setups it knows, and an unknown `<name>.setup` offers its literal words as candidates, of which the existing probe keeps the one the evaluated version follows; where two move the version, it refuses, as now. A down payment on layer 1's probe-located revision and checksums. And and `checksums` refreshes every distfile whose name the version derives, each fetched from the plan MacPorts makes. They're among the most used ports, with a release a month.
     - **checksums on just about anything** (the person's word, 2026-10-09, on field testing's list: "`checksums` should be able to update or verify checksums on just about anything, and shouldn't be subject to most hold conditions"). Update: `checksums <port>` rewrites the checksums of what the Portfile as it stands fetches, on every platform it models, by MacPorts' own fetch plan for it, with no version probe and no version input, so cpi's (qmake5), garage's (gitea.setup), go-1.26's and go-1.27's (go_toolchain.setup), and their like go: today the input checksums loads first (portedit's `sourceInput`) reaches the probe. It regenerates crates and Go modules from the new source; where an override or a patch can't be regenerated, as certstrap's, rust-glancer's, meli's, and fnox's submodules, it writes the rest and names what it left, rather than refusing the port. Verify: `checksums --plan` is it, no new flag, saying "all N distfiles match", or naming each that differs, with an exit status of its own, over every modeled platform's distfiles. Holds: none of update's, majors, moves, owner changes, or an inconclusive probe; it stops only where a download fails, and writes no value it didn't compute from one; a stealth change is still said loudly, with today's revbump and dist_subdir (portedit/stealth.go). It covers the Go toolchain item's checksums half, and the known issue of regenerating crates and modules after a hand edit.
   - **How a command asks and exits**, one batch, second in the release (the command package review, [review](../reviews/2026-10-15-command-package-review.md), coalesced the same day at the person's "Send it", 07:13Z; C2a's unattended holds, the Branches items' question, build profiles, `ship`, and part 3's offers all read it). One `decide(question, default)` that asks on a terminal, takes `--yes` in a script, and otherwise refuses in one sentence with exit 1, and says how it was answered, asked, `--yes`, or nobody there, so holds and `build.unattended` read the same fact (decision 3 takes `build.unattended` for `ship --yes` even on a terminal, so "is there a terminal" is the wrong question). bump's `streams.unattended()` and submit's `unattended` move onto it. It converts only the 15 `terminal()` decisions and 22 prompts that survive `ship`, leaving the 3 decisions and 5 prompts in code `ship` deletes; folds in the review's 2b TTY predicates and the seven no-terminal refusals, two of which exit 0 today (clean.go:161, reviewpr.go:59); and puts exit constants at the 9 surviving sites and root.go:304, so check.go:173 stops branching on the number 2.
   - **b. Typed holds (C2a), then the majors hold.** serve, status, and bump pass `[]model.Concern`, not `[]string` (holds are strings in five places today, as servesubmit.go:111 and status.go:59); serve dedupes holds by `Key()`, and the JSON gains `concerns` beside `held`. The majors hold is then a Concern with a `major-version` rule, returned before the edit so nothing builds, its key in a `[holds]` table, where the hold list will be, not under `update.`: It gains "a hold is said once", the second half of the py-kubernetes entry below, as a renderer grouping `[]model.Concern` by key without the port, and with it `engine.ComparisonLines{Hold, Ports, Words}`, which ends the `"! "` prefix sliced back off at submit.go:520: the port grouping written once (the command package review's row 4).
     - **bump and serve hold a major version for a look** (field testing at b8baa26b, 2026-10-07; the person chose v0.3.1 the same day). `dockhand bump hyperfine` went from 1.21.0 to 2.0.0 and opened macports/macports-ports#35353 without a pause, where the person's preference since 2026-10-06 is to hold off on major bumps: update's batch flags a major, and bump and serve's submit don't stop for one. A setting, `update.majors = "hold"`, under which bump and serve hold an update whose major version moves, with a `!` line naming both versions and the `dockhand submit --branch …` to run after a look; and `outdated` marks the updates it would hold. Built as a concern in the hold list serve's submit reads (servesubmit's concerns), not in bump's own path, so v0.4.0's holds and `ship` read the same list (the prioritization thread, 2026-10-09).
   - **c. The fixes, grouped by the code they touch.**
     - *Check and evidence:*
       - **An untracked file in a changed port's directory is said before the VM boots,** as a new patch in `files/` is, with what keeps it: `--include <path>` for this check, or `git add`. Today it's left out of the check silently.
       - **A kept dependency archive that's gone is named, and its record dropped.** Before a guest is given them, dependency archives whose file is missing or changed are already skipped (batch 90), silently, so the record stays and each check skips it again. The skip names the port and drops the record. Attempt 1 of check-264 failing after the boot, where attempt 2 recovered, isn't explained by that skip, which 464d583c already had; it needs that run's logs to say why.
       - **A dependency's kept archive lasts past the day's cleanup** (the rc9 full stage, 2026-10-08: kept archives went from 47 to 17 in a run, by design). A target's archive is kept while it's its newest passed build's, or an open branch's; a dependency's, which batch 90 keeps so a guest-built toolchain builds once, is named by no result, so each cleanup forgets it once it's an hour old (`archiveGrace`, `PruneArchives`), and serve cleans daily: rust built from source on a release MacPorts has no archives for is built again the next day. The newest of each port's in each environment stays until `cleanup.after`, as a result's record does. With it, SQL-S4, `KeepDependency` in one transaction, and SQL-O5, `keptArchive` read inside reuse's View; the rule itself is in v0.3.1's keep-set.
       - **check and serve refuse a Tart older than 2.39.0, as setup does** (the rc9 full stage's F8, 2026-10-08). With a Tart reporting 2.38.0, check-100 started a VM and passed: the minimum, for openai/tart#1344's listing blocked by a running ASIF VM, is held in setup and provisioning alone (tart/provision/vm.go). check and serve's checks refuse it in the same words, before a VM starts. Not for the release: since batch 115 a listing that hangs is bounded and said.
       - **A GitHub tag that moved after its check is said, by check and submit** (the rc8 full stage's D-N5, 2026-10-07; the person chose v0.3.1). dh-fixture-retag was checked at v1.0.0, 1d15bc8, and the tag was then moved to d4c0fdb: `check --plan` offered check-41's pass for reuse, and submit's preview read "Checks: passed (check-41)", saying nothing of the tag, where GitHub's v1.0.0 archive no longer matches the Portfile's checksums. Only Git-fetched ports have their source recorded at plan time and read again (`movedSources`). The plan records the commit of each GitHub tag a target's archives are fetched at, by `git ls-remote`, which spends no API budget, beside its `Git` sources; a check doesn't reuse a build whose tag has moved since, and says so; and `movedSources` reads those tags again for submit's preview, which holds, "v1.0.0 now names d4c0fdb, not 1d15bc8, as when check-41 built it; dockhand checksums <port> handles a stealth update". Plans are JSON, so no schema changes. About one batch, with tests for each. Built on the existing seams: plans are JSON, so no schema change.
       - **A migration under a running dockhand, and a dead run counted as running** (the v0.3.1 port update, 2026-10-15). Done 2026-10-16 ([note](2026-10-16-runs-migrations-images.md)). A main build migrated the database to 032 while v0.3.1's check-366 waited for a VM; v0.3.1 then couldn't write its heartbeat ("unusable database … schema 32, newer than this dockhand supports (31)"), said that after 2m "another dockhand may take over its work", and exited 0, its check left recorded as running. Main's `check` then refused a new one, "check-366 is already running for these files; dockhand wait check-366 follows it", while `status` said "Checks stopped (check-366)" with `Next: dockhand wait check-366`, and `logs` said running; `dockhand cancel check-366` cleared it. Two fixes. A migration that finds a dockhand on the older schema with a fresh heartbeat says so and asks, "2 dockhand processes on schema 31 are running …; migrate anyway?" (field testing's suggestion), refusing in a script; and an older build that finds the schema newer ends its run as interrupted, saying why, rather than as a lost heartbeat a live run could be mistaken for. And a stopped run is said as stopped, with how to resume it, everywhere: field testing hit the same on the rust check that night, where after check-365's process got SIGINT, `status` said stopped, `check --head` refused, "check-365 is already running for these files", and `dockhand wait check-365` resumed it. `wait` is the way back and nothing says so: `status` says "stopped; dockhand wait check-365 resumes it", `check`'s refusal says the run is stopped and offers `wait` to resume it or `--replace` to start over, and `logs` says stopped rather than running.
       - **An image whose MacPorts is behind this Mac's is said, by `status` and `setup`** (the person's "yes", 2026-10-16, after the dockhand 0.3.1 port update). Done 2026-10-16 ([note](2026-10-16-runs-migrations-images.md)). An image keeps the MacPorts it was made with: v0.3.1's setup takes whatever MacPorts' installer updates itself to, but nothing says when a kept image falls behind. #35553 was checked on dockhand-xcode-tahoe's 2.12.6 while this Mac and MacPorts CI had 2.12.7, and its "Tested on" line said 2.12.6 with no word. The image's manifest has its `macports_version` and the host's evaluator reports `BaseVersion`; where the image's is older, `setup`'s health report and `status`'s provider line name it, with `dockhand setup tart <release> --rebuild` (and `--xcode` for an Xcode image) as the way forward. A check still runs on it.
       - **A newer released MacPorts minor is admitted with a warning, not refused** (the same "yes"). Done 2026-10-16 ([note](2026-10-16-runs-migrations-images.md)). `admitBase` (macports/eval/compatibility.go) admits only the 2.11 and 2.12 families, so on the day 2.13.0 ships, every command that evaluates Portfiles refuses until a dockhand release adds it. A released family newer than `supportedFamilies` is admitted, with one line on standard error per command, "MacPorts Base 2.13.0 is newer than this dockhand was built against (2.11 and 2.12); results may be wrong where Base changed", and the version recorded with a check's evidence as now. Development builds stay behind the preview adapter, and an older family stays refused; a startup check that fails is still a refusal, whatever the version.
     - *Submit:*
       - **submit's `--json` result names who requested changes, and whether each was asked to review again** (the rc8 full stage's B4, 2026-10-07). Done 2026-10-15, with `Submitted.Edited` (82d75484, [note](2026-10-15-submit-result.md)). B4 updated #17 in place, and @herbygillot's "changes requested" and the re-request offer went only to the text output (`applySubmit`, command/submit.go), "@… requested changes; ask them to review again on GitHub, or set submit.rerequest_review = \"always\"", so a script reading the envelope never learned of either. The result carries `changes_requested_by` and, for each, `rerequested`. With it, B4 asserts both from the envelope, and from a text run's output, where the offer is printed: on stdout, not stderr, so the row reads that. In one batch with `Submitted.Edited` from `ApplySubmit` (the command package review): both change `applySubmit` and its result.
       - **submit's preview names the account that opens the pull request** (the rc9 full stage's D-C4, 2026-10-08). Done 2026-10-15, with `github.LoginStatus`, `setup --json`, and `auth` deleted (c10e2ea4, [note](2026-10-15-login-named.md)); the fork another account owns is a warning, and stays one, at the person's "Keep the warning" (2026-10-15 21:34Z). With another account's token in GH_TOKEN, `submit --plan` previewed "From herbyg-test/macports-ports:dockhand/… PR: opens a new one" and named no account, though auth status named herbygillot; nothing was pushed. The preview and `--json` name the account and where its login comes from (GH_TOKEN, GITHUB_TOKEN, the Keychain, the GitHub CLI). Where the fork's owner check gives way, under DOCKHAND_PULL_REQUESTS's sandbox (engine.fork, submit.go) and with `--remote`, a login that isn't the head repository's owner is said, a warning, or refused where the person decides it should be. Where the login comes from is `github.LoginStatus` (the split's 7a row 2) (the command package split, coalesced 2026-10-15 at the person's "Send, split in v0.4.0", 20:52Z; the project's plan/command-package-split-coalesced-2026-10-15.md), and it is also what replaces `auth status --json`: `setup --json`, new (root.go's `--json` allowlist doesn't take setup today), whose record is `SetupReport` (7b row 3, landing here; part 3 keeps only `engine.Setup`'s move and the `setup` verb cut). Then `auth` is deleted, and its acceptance rows re-pointed: A2, B7, C1, and F5 run `auth status`, which becomes `setup --json`, or `setup` where a person reads it; D-C1, D-C2, and D-C5 run `auth logout`, which becomes `setup github --logout`. README.md:118's v0.3.0 known issue, which names `dockhand auth status` as the workaround, goes with the fix.
       - **A Python build file whose change is only the version holds nothing, and a hold is said once** (field testing at f41fbad6, 2026-10-10). Done 2026-10-15 (4ba5aae6, [note](2026-10-15-setup-py-version.md)). py-kubernetes 37.0.0 to 37.0.1 was held, "! setup.py changed; the build may need the Portfile to follow", where setup.py's whole change was `CLIENT_VERSION = "37.0.0"` to `"37.0.1"`. A build file's change that's the project's version alone, as configure.ac's and CMakeLists.txt's are read, is said and holds nothing: setup.py's and pyproject.toml's too, read as a change whose every changed line differs only by the old version written as the new, which covers a constant such as CLIENT_VERSION as well as `version=`. And submit's line said the reason twice, once for each subport ("…follow; upstream's setup.py changed; …follow"): one reason is said once. Its second half, a hold said once, is built in b, with C2a.
       - **A distfile MacPorts doesn't extract is compared as the file it is** (field testing at 285a496f, 2026-10-12; bashunit 0.51.0 to 0.52.0, #35444 submitted by hand after LICENSE was read). Done 2026-10-15, with the next item (b495055a, [note](2026-10-15-distfile-and-patch.md)). bashunit fetches one shell script, with `extract.only` empty and the script copied into worksrcpath, and update held it, "Upstream archives not compared: … archive/tar: invalid tar header". Batch 73's fix for 1password-cli (field testing's batch 10, finding 2) sniffs a file's first bytes for an installer package or a Mach-O executable (`archive.ErrBinary`), which a script isn't. Which distfiles are archives is MacPorts' to say: one the evaluated port doesn't extract (not in `extract.only`) is read as a single file, and compared as one, the old script against the new, holding nothing for being no archive; sniffing stays for a binary that is extracted. Where the single file carries no license, as a script often does, the forge's tag archive, which the port doesn't fetch, could serve the license comparison; that's a choice to make when it's built, not assumed.
       - **A golang port's patch is checked where MacPorts applies it** (field testing at a9cef1f8, 2026-10-09; maddy 0.9.5 to 0.9.6, #35417). Done 2026-10-15 (b495055a): maddy's worksrcdir is GOPATH's with extract.rename, which patchcheck read as a subdirectory below the archive's top; herdr's is `patch.args -p1`, which wasn't read. update said patch-build.sh.diff doesn't apply to 0.9.6's source, "No file to patch. Skipping…; 2 out of 2 hunks ignored while patching './build.sh'", and check-308 built 0.9.6 with it applied: the check ran the patch where build.sh isn't. Batch 80 read a golang port's worksrcdir, `gopath/src/<package>`, which no archive holds, as the archive's top directory, for lima; maddy is a golang port too, with no go.vendors, and its tag takes a `v` prefix (`go.setup … 0.9.5 v`), so what differs is to be found first. The check runs patch in the directory, and at the -p level, MacPorts' patch phase would, which the evaluation gives as `patch.dir` and `patch.pre_args`. Not golang's alone (field testing at 5eb101a3, herdr 0.9.3 to 0.9.5, #35519 to #35527's batch): herdr, a Rust port, was warned that patch-macports-update.diff doesn't apply, "No file to patch… 7 out of 7 hunks ignored while patching 'a/src/update.rs'", where the check built with it; the probe applied a Git-style patch at -p0 where MacPorts uses -p1. So the probe applies every port's patches from worksrcpath with the evaluated `patch.pre_args` and `patch.args`, lima's, maddy's, and herdr's one cause.
     - *Serve:*
       - **serve's daily look notifies, when what it found changed** (the rc10 full stage's B7, 2026-10-09). Done 2026-10-15 (b3a56eee, [note](2026-10-15-look-notifies.md)), naming `outdated --mine`, which outlives part 2. The look's count, as "116 ports of yours have newer releases", goes to serve.log and status alone: serve notifies for a pull request's change, a branch it prepared, and a submission, so a serve running as an agent with `serve.for_outdated = "list"`, the default, says nothing a person sees. One notification a look, where the ports it found differ from the last look's: "your ports: 116 have newer releases (dockhand update --outdated --mine)".
       - **serve --install and --uninstall wait for launchd** (the rc10 full stage's B7, 2026-10-09; rc6's too). Done 2026-10-15, with the agent, `Notify`, and `Open` in `macos` and tart/host's `Start`/`Stop` gone (988d8299, [note](2026-10-15-launchd-wait.md)). `launchctl bootout` returns before launchd has unloaded the agent, so a status run at once reads serve's agent still there, and --install's bootstrap after a bootout can meet the job still loaded. Both read `launchctl print gui/<uid>/<label>` until the job is gone, up to thirty seconds, and say it where it isn't. B7's harness waits meanwhile. The pattern is already in tart/host/launchd.go:73, and B7's harness wait goes with it. With the wait, the launchd agent moves to `macos` (the split's 7a row 1): `macos.LaunchAgent.Install` and `.Remove` take the wait, and `Notify` and `Open` ride along (rows 1 and 7, the same `macos.Command` seam); tart/host's uncalled v2 `Start`/`Stop` go, kept by PR #4 only as the wait's pattern.
     - *Status and outdated* (the last two kept minimal, since layer 1's readiness rewrites status's verdict; and with them, from the command package review: `OutdatedPort.Class()` and `OutdatedReport.Summary()`, built by the grouped-reasons, unpublished-release, and daily-look items, for plain `outdated`, moved from v0.5.0; and the three pull-request observation enums in `model`, defined with "status reads PR CI itself", whose code SQL-O8 rewrites, engine/follow.go, with their 8 reading sites converted):
       - **A command that waits says on what, after a few seconds, without -v** (field testing at c9ad2112, 2026-10-08). Done 2026-10-15 (d4ae0824, [note](2026-10-15-waits-said.md)): the index cache's lock, a port's lookup, and outdated's count off a terminal. The first `dockhand outdated flyctl oh-my-posh` after a rebuild printed nothing for 13 minutes and was killed; run again with -vv it took seconds, the PortIndex an incremental pass of 3 changed paths, and the GitHub quota was full, so not a rate limit. Read at c9ad2112: a full PortIndex build ("Building the PortIndex; this may take several minutes") and waiting on another process's indexing ("Waiting for another process to finish indexing this source") are said already, but the index cache's shared lock (`portindex/cache.go`, `filelock.Acquire` Shared) is waited on unsaid, as is any network call. What it waited on is unknown. A command whose step has run some seconds says the step, "waiting for the PortIndex cache, which pid N holds" or "waiting on github.com for flyctl's releases", once, and outdated says its count of ports looked up as it goes, as it does on a terminal.
       - **The ports outdated couldn't check are said by their reasons** (the rc10 full stage's B7, 2026-10-09).        - **An unpublished release is said by outdated as update says it** (field testing at 43949b89, 2026-10-09, the second of three).        - **A branch started for a port is that port's branch** (the command-line UX second look's §1, 2026-10-07; added for v0.3.1 on 2026-10-10, the person's "Go ahead", since `ship -p` builds on it). `start --port jq` makes a branch that `check -p jq` can't find, "no tracked branch changes jq", and `edit jq` from the checkout's root starts a second one beside it; `status --port jq` with two such branches says there are none. A branch's ports come only from what it changes (`BranchesChanging`), so the ports it was started for are recorded nowhere. The branch's record keeps its intended ports, `start --port`'s and the port an authoring verb started it for, and `-p`, an authoring verb's auto-start, `status --port`, and the PORTS column read them beside what it changes.
       - **`-b` names an existing branch; `--new=<name>` starts one** (the same review's §1 and §3; added for v0.3.1 on 2026-10-10, as `ship -b` builds on it). `edit jq -b jq-typo` said "Started dockhand/jq-typo" with no question: `-b` on an authoring verb starts a branch when none has the name, so a typo makes one. `-b` takes only an existing branch, everywhere, refusing "no branch jq-typo; --new=jq-typo starts one", and `--new=<name>` names a new branch, with `--new` alone still choosing the name.

       - **Two false refusals, and an uncertain tag's hint** (field testing at c97bca63, 2026-10-09; rust-glancer #35397 and certstrap #35398 went up by hand). (1) rust-glancer was refused, "edition is pinned to Git rev …; cargo.crates_github declares branches only", though its Portfile sets `cargo.offline_cmd {--locked}`, which resolves Git sources online: depblock reads only an empty `cargo.offline_cmd` as online (`gitPolicy`, git.go, and the cargo check in blocks.go, two copies of one rule). A value without `--offline` or `--frozen` is online, by one function in depblock both read; and the refusal came from checking the current version's source, so a port that builds on master was refused before its update was tried, which the same rule settles. (2) certstrap was refused, "existing go.vendors differs from the original manifest/helper output", for go-md2man/v2 and blackfriday/v2, which its Portfile names without the `/v2` older go2port wrote: a module `X` declared where the generator has `X/vN`, at the same lock, is the same row, not an override. (3) certgraph is set aside rightly, its newest-comparing tag on an older commit, but the hint, `dockhand update certgraph 20220513.20220514.0` "after a look", would better name a `livecheck.regex` that leaves the stray tag out.
