Every version update removes a top-level `dist_subdir ${name}/${version}_${revision}`, or `_N`, the forms MacPorts' guide gives a stealth update, without looking at the distfiles (`portedit`'s `dropStealthDistSubdir`). yq's man page keeps one name across versions, which the line keeps apart on the mirrors. Without it, check-27 failed at checksum on Tart, while GitHub, fetching from GitHub, passed, so a check on GitHub alone would have let it be submitted. The removal is `portedit`'s, which has both versions' fetch plans: it keeps the line where any distfile's name is the same in both. Done 2026-09-29 ([note](2026-09-29-a-dist-subdir-still-needed.md));
  - the removal said in `update --plan`'s text, as the update says it (finding 2). The plan shows only the diff's `-` line. Done 2026-09-29 (the same note).
- **What the git run asked for** ([review](../reviews/2026-09-28-hugo-bump-exercise.md#git-a-port-dockhand-couldnt-update)):
  - A hand-made version bump that tidy couldn't name (finding 5) is item 6's Portfile inspection, done 2026-09-30 ([note](2026-09-30-portfile-inspection.md)): the version is read from the first forge setup line anywhere, here git-devel's `github.setup` in its subport, so git's own `version` line was never read.
- **What the ov run asked for** ([review](../reviews/2026-09-28-hugo-bump-exercise.md#ov-through-adopt-edit-retry-and-submit---passing)):
  - **`go.toolchain_min` written as go.mod's `go` directive gives it,** `1.26.8` rather than `1.26` (finding 2, and the tbls run's 1). Done 2026-09-29 ([note](2026-09-29-go-minimum-as-go-mod-writes-it.md)). It was a matter of fidelity, not the defect this item once called it: the Go PortGroup compares only the series, since MacPorts ships the newest patch release of each series, so `1.26` gated tbls exactly as `1.26.8` does, and comparing by series is right. What was a defect was the `toolchain` line read as a requirement, though Go documents it as a suggestion and the PortGroup builds with `GOTOOLCHAIN=local`: it could gate a port on systems where it builds;
  - `submit --passing --yes` refused, rather than `--yes` silently ignored (finding 7). Done 2026-09-29, as D11 decided ([note](2026-09-29-passing-takes-no-yes.md)).
- **What the gh, usql, hk, and pgdog run asked for** ([review](../reviews/2026-09-28-hugo-bump-exercise.md#gh-usql-hk-and-pgdog-chosen-for-untried-paths)):
  - **a dependency read as MacPorts reads it** (finding 5). Done 2026-09-29 ([note](2026-09-29-read-as-base-reads-it.md)): `macports.ParseDependency` admits what Base's own two patterns admit, checked against Base's validator, and names the last field as the port. The evaluator refused mise's `port:bin/cmake:cmake`, which Base accepts: `portdepends::validate_depends_options` takes `port`, anything, then the port's name as the last field, while `eval/evaluator.go` allowed `port` only with one field after it;
  - **a license change that only moves a copyright year doesn't hold** (finding 1). Done 2026-09-29 ([note](2026-09-29-read-as-base-reads-it.md)): where a license file's lines differ only in the years of copyright lines, it's said with the line, without holding; a program's source named for a license, such as usql's generated `text/license.go`, is no license file. Every changed license file held (`sourcecompare`), so usql's `LICENSE`, "2016-2025" to "2016-2026", held `bump`;
- **What a real rebase of gh asked for** ([review](../reviews/2026-09-28-hugo-bump-exercise.md#submitting-hk-and-gh-with-a-real-rebase)):
  - **status credits a check of the files as they are, whichever it was** (finding 2). Status judges only the branch's latest check (`engine/status.go`), so after `restore rebase-29` put gh back at exactly check-35's files, it said "passed for older work" of check-37's rebased ones. Submit reads the newest finished check of the tree (`EvidenceFor`) and would have credited check-35, so the two disagree. Status should read what submit reads, and name the latest check only where none covers the files. Done 2026-09-29 ([note](2026-09-29-checks-of-the-files-as-they-are.md));
  - rebase's next step from what covers the new files (finding 3). "Next: dockhand check, since the files it builds on have changed" is printed after every rebase (`command/verbs.go`), even where a check of the rebased files already stands, as it did for the second rebase onto the same master. Done 2026-09-29 (the same note): it names the check and says what status would;
  - the plan saying GitHub's results aren't reused (finding 1), with item 6's environment identity above. Done 2026-09-29 (the same note), as the check runs rather than in the plan, which doesn't ask providers what they are: an environment whose provider can't say builds every target, and says so where another of the check's environments could reuse.



## Reviews: the triage of each, as the roadmap had it

## Reviews

**The [code-organization review](../reviews/2026-09-27-code-organization-review.md)** of 2026-09-27 read `a71fc67f`. Its 46 findings were checked again at `3b16b187` ([note](2026-09-27-code-organization-review-reconciled.md)).
- **Taken:**
  - before item 6: findings 1, 2, 9, 13, 17 (`ExitCode`), 18, 19 (the embedding), 20, 24, 31, 32, 33, 34, 35, 36 (the regression), 37, 38 (the unreachable path), 42, 43, and 46;
  - inside item 6: 1 (the dependencies), 4, 25, 27, 28, 36, and 39;
  - inside item 7: 6, 8, and 9 (the subject);
  - the rest as smaller items.
- **Worse than it said, through later work:** 1, 32, 35, 36, and 37. Two regressions were fixed at once.
- **Changed:**
  - 2: a misspelled `--tests` is refused; `bump` and serve don't bypass the rule; `update --submit` does.
  - 6 is P3.
  - 18: the `deadcode` advice.
  - 26: the merge loop stays.
  - 42: signal the group gently, minding a prompting `sudo`.
- **Declined:**
  - splitting `store` or `portindex` (23);
  - finding 21's plist half;
  - 41's legacy-plan deletion;
  - 20's alias and shared registry;
  - a branch shape shared across commands (10): the person's rule is one shape per command, which commands needn't share.
- **Decided by the person:** D4, that what an update couldn't check holds; D5, that the history verbs stay in the engine.

**The [architecture and data-flow review](../reviews/2026-09-27-architecture-and-data-flow.md)** of 2026-09-27 was checked against the code at `52d03e2a`.
- **Its probes:** all seven fail as stated.
- **Its measurements hold:** `engine` is 30 files and 9,329 lines, with 29 local imports; all three providers import it; `tools/survey` needs `assess` and `tools/stateperf` needs `state` and `workflow`.
- **Its claims hold,** in particular that tidy and rebase move Git refs inside database transactions.

Taken:
- **Findings 1 to 3,** as items 1 to 3.
- **Finding 4,** as item 4.
- **Finding 7,** as item 5. It corrected the previous roadmap, whose removal list would have broken the survey.
- **Finding 8,** as a smaller item.

Changed:

## From Next on 2026-10-17: v0.4.0's parts, and v0.5.0 as it stood

When Codex's architecture review was taken and the roadmap coalesced around it (2026-10-17, [note](2026-10-17-codex-review-coalesced.md)), Next's text from its "Now" line to its "Then" item moved here whole, as it stood at a268b6e5, with its links pointed from here. v0.4.0's three parts were done; v0.5.0's items 5.1 to 5.9 are the roadmap's six parts now, each saying which it was.

**Now: v0.4.0's acceptance.** v0.3.1 was tagged 2026-10-15 on 147dc4a9 and announced; macports/macports-ports#35553 updated the port to it through dockhand, merged 2026-10-16. v0.4.0's three parts are done on main (below, and in [the roadmap's history](2026-10-16-roadmap-history.md)), with the rc1 full stage's findings and Codex's [architecture review](../reviews/2026-10-16-architecture-832f059.md)'s high 1 and medium 4 (e2dcb9b4); its high 2 and high 3 are v0.5.0's, in items 5.1 and step 3. v0.4.0-rc3 (09de54ca, tagged 2026-10-17) is in acceptance on the M1.

**After the release: two releases to the final state** (the coalesced roadmap, the project's plan/coalesced-roadmap-2026-10-13.md, from a survey of the code and every open item at 304e8d42; the person's "yes" to its order, 2026-10-13 17:03Z, "though I do wish it was more compressed", and "Two releases" on the card that followed, 17:05Z). First, v0.3.1, a point release of the SQL study's SQL-S1 on the quick stage (the study folded in the same evening, at the person's "if … certain things can go in sooner … we should do it", 18:39Z). Then what the coalesced roadmap called v0.3.1, v0.4.0, and v0.4.1 ships as one v0.4.0, about 19 batches in the order below (17 before the command package split was placed in it, 21 with its first wave, which moved to v0.5.0 at the person's adoption of the step-back review's recommendations, 2026-10-15), and layer 1 is v0.5.0: two full prime-time passes. A harm found in v0.3.0 gets a v0.3.x point release on the quick stage alone, and field testing exercises main between tags. The old modes go out with `ship`, since no compatibility period is owed, so each file is opened once and the acceptance rows rewritten once; holds are typed before `ship` (C2a), so the majors hold, the new holds, and `ship --yes` are written once against the type layer 1 keeps; and what layer 1 rewrites anyway (most of M1, the local "what changed" logic) waits for it. The architecture review's codes are written A-H1 and so on here, apart from the acceptance harness's harm invariants H1 to H9, and the SQL study's ([review](../reviews/2026-10-13-sql-study.md)) SQL-S1 to SQL-S5 and SQL-O1 to SQL-O8, apart from prime-time's S1 soak. The study's "not taken" stay out: no rewrite of the N+1 reads or the polling for speed; no ORM, query builder, or generated code; and no plan cache, prepared-statement cache, or second durability mode.

Decided with the order, at the person's "2-5, go with your recommendations" (2026-10-13 17:57Z), and built into part 2: `ship --passing` shows one plan listing every branch with its holds and asks once on a terminal, a script needing `--yes` (amending D11); the evidence a submission requires is the build profile in effect, `build.default` when a person runs it and `build.unattended` when `ship --yes` or serve submits (replacing D17's `check.on`); a batch `ship --to submit` queues its checks and submits each branch as its check passes, and a rerun resumes it, as serve's drain does; and a build-time download is a notice, not a hold. Still open: D-R3, and the privacy follow-up.

1. **v0.3.1** and 2. **v0.4.0, part 1** are done; both are kept whole in [the roadmap's history](2026-10-16-roadmap-history.md). Its last item, D-R3's, done at the person's decision:
     - **A deleted base image's refusal says it's a restore** (the rc9 full stage's D-R3, 2026-10-08). With dockhand-base-golden-gate gone and its golden copy kept, check says "dockhand setup tart golden-gate makes dockhand-base-golden-gate", which reads as an hour and 60 GB, where setup restores it from the golden copy in about 70 seconds. Where the golden copy is there, it says so: "dockhand setup tart golden-gate restores it from dockhand-golden-golden-gate, in about a minute". check doesn't restore it itself (the person's decision on D-R3, 2026-10-16: it would write tens of gigabytes unasked). Done 2026-10-16.
3. **v0.4.0, part 2: build profiles, holds, `ship`, and the final command surface** (about 6 batches, in this order, so each file is opened once; the person's "v0.4.0 after", 2026-10-09, for ship). Not taken, as the command package review says: no workflow or pipeline type, no command registry, and no branch shape shared across commands. The package split is taken, in the order the split's coalescing set (the command package split, coalesced 2026-10-15 at the person's "Send, split in v0.4.0", 20:52Z; the project's plan/command-package-split-coalesced-2026-10-15.md): a package is cut once the judgments in it have moved and its imports point one way, so `term` at the end of part 1, `compose` with X1 in part 3, and the rest in v0.5.0, right after 5.5 (the step-back review's recommendation 3, adopted 2026-10-15).0, right after 5.5.
   1. **Build profiles** (done 2026-10-16, [note](2026-10-16-build-profiles.md); the check standing judged against build.default's environments until ship brings decision 3) (the person's design, 2026-10-13, on the prioritization thread; first, since `ship`'s check reads `build.unattended`). A profile is a provider and the releases it builds on, kept in config under `build.profiles`; its releases are a list, as `["15", "26"]`, or a rule. The built-in profiles all run on Tart: `host`, alias `local`, this Mac's release; `minmax`, each port's oldest and newest supported release that dockhand runs, macOS 12 at the earliest, where a run of several ports covers their union and each port builds on its own two alone; `macports-ci`, the releases MacPorts' Actions workflow at master names, less those the port's platforms exclude (what `--on ci` reads today, engine/ci.go); and `all`, every release Tart runs that the port supports. A person defines their own, as `ci-exact = { provider = "github", releases = "macports-ci" }`. Settings: `build.default = "host"`, and `build.unattended = "minmax"` for ship, serve, and bump submitting with nobody looking. `--build <profile>` goes on check and ship, and conflicts with `--on`, which stays explicit environments only. `--on ci` and `check.on` go, with no aliases, as dockhand is unreleased and bridges between its versions don't matter (the person, 2026-10-13). The result tables, the pull request's and the terminal's, show only the releases something was built on, or excluded for a reason of the port's own; the releases minmax chose not to build on leave them (the person's word, 2026-10-16). With it, dockhand's corrections to its own edits held to one fidelity check (the same review, high 3). Removing a patch upstream merged (portedit/patches.go) checks only the patch list, the version, and the revision before it replaces `Prepared`. The Go toolchain and stealth-directory corrections, by contrast, compare the whole family against an explicit allowed change and record a report. So a setting that depends on the patch list can change unsaid, such as `use_autoreconf` or a build dependency inside `if {[llength ${patchfiles}] > 0}`, or a sibling subport sharing the list. One validated-correction operation in portedit owns four things: the snapshot before, the candidate's evaluation, the whole-family comparison against the allowed change, and publishing the files, snapshot, and report together. Patch removal moves onto it first, then the toolchain and stealth corrections.

### Now in v0.5.0

- To part 1, item 1, as an added program said rather than held: **A workspace member the Portfile doesn't build, still said** (a repeat of batch 13's second finding): epic-lore's "lore-stamp added: the Portfile's destroot may name the old ones", where the Portfile builds only lore-client and lore-server. The lib-only filter is to be checked against members the Portfile doesn't build.
- To part 3, item 1: **update moves a commit pin.** wezterm pins github.setup to a commit, its version derived from the commit's date and hash; outdated says its branch moved past the pin, and update refuses both the version and the commit, so it's bumped by hand. update would take the branch's head, or a commit named, write the hash into github.setup, and the version by the port's scheme where it can read it, else take it named. Since batch 97 outdated says the edit that works.
- To part 1, item 5: **status says what clean kept of a cleaned branch** (the rc6 full stage's C7, 2026-10-06). A merged branch whose fork branch moved after the merge keeps that fork branch through `clean`, which says so as it runs; `status --all` then reads only "cleaned". It would say "cleaned (fork branch kept: moved after merge)", which needs clean to record what it kept with the branch, where now it journals only what it removed, and status has no per-branch reading of the journal.
- To part 4, item 1, as a refusal's network kind: **Offline said as offline** (the rc6 full stage's D-N1, 2026-10-07). outdated and update offline exit 1 naming the fetch failure, nothing changed, but in git's words, "git fetch: … Connection reset by peer"; a lead line, "couldn't reach GitHub (connection reset); nothing was changed", would read better.

## From Next and the decisions on 2026-10-24: the modeline's tidy left out

Implementation found that tidy giving its commits the modeline left a tree no check of the working files matched (2f49a0be's [note](2026-10-24-modeline.md)), and the person chose to leave tidy out ("Let's go with your recommendation.", 2026-10-24 05:39Z). Before that, Next's entry read, whole:

- **Every Portfile dockhand touches gets MacPorts' modeline** (the person's "I'd like `dockhand` to add or correct the modeline on the Portfiles it touches by default. We'll add a flag to allow the user to explicitly keep it from doing so.", 2026-10-24 04:41Z; about half a batch). Only `create` writes the modeline today (portcreate.go `Modeline`, the line `port lint --nitpick` expects). Every Portfile dockhand writes or commits, an update's, bump's, ship's, and serve's edits, `--revbump-dependents`' revisions, and the Portfiles `tidy` commits, gets that line first: added where line 1 is no modeline, and replacing a modeline whose settings are the same, spelled otherwise (`vim:fenc=utf-8:filetype=tcl`, a missing `mode: tcl`, `vim: fenc`). A modeline whose settings differ, tabs or another width, is the maintainer's choice and stays, since the file's indentation follows it. In a ports tree of 2026-10-02, of 20,129 Portfiles, 17,629 have the line as `create` writes it, 1,306 have none, 1,169 spell the same settings otherwise, and 19 set others. It's portedit's correction allowing no change (correction.go, `allowed` nil), or proven by the parse alone, since line 1 is a comment, whichever costs less; it rides the edit's own commit. Each command that writes or commits a Portfile takes `--keep-modeline`, which leaves line 1 as it was, serve's applying to every update it makes. Done when an update of a port with no modeline adds it, one spelled otherwise is replaced, one with tabs stays, `tidy` of a hand-edited Portfile adds it, and `--keep-modeline` changes nothing; the corpus, comparing version lines, checksums, and dependency blocks, is unchanged.

The decided entry read:

- **dockhand adds or corrects the modeline on every Portfile it touches, unless told to keep it** (the person's ask, 2026-10-24 04:41Z): a fix for main riding the release after v0.5.0, with `--keep-modeline` to opt out.

## From Next, Then, and Later on 2026-10-24: the bazel session's notes

The bazel session's notes ([note](2026-10-24-field-notes-coalesced.md)) joined the two sessions' coalesced the same evening. The Tart entry 67f37e9d placed was wrong: it read `providers.tart.capacity` as what runs a check's environments in turn, where a check runs two VMs and the capacity counts checks. Before the bazel notes, these read, whole:

Found in two sessions' notes, gathered by field testing on 2026-10-24 at the person's asks, the gnome-voice session's ("Check in with the session … for its Dockhand experiences.", 21:04Z) and the exiv2 session's on rc5+388c304c ("Get some notes from … as well", 21:07Z), each a fix for main riding v0.6.0, buildable now, never an rc of v0.5.0's, and none harm by the release bar. What was already fixed, what went to Then and Later, and what stays out is in [the coalescing note](activity/2026-10-24-field-notes-coalesced.md):

- **A development build asks before it migrates a release's database, and a newer schema's refusal says whose it is** (the exiv2 session's notes, verified, with the gnome-voice session's note 4, verified; about half a batch). The exiv2 session's read-only `status -b`, on a main build, migrated the shared database from schema 32 to 39 at 13:25:34, as `engine.Open` does for any command, saying so and keeping the schema 32 copy (engine.go), and the installed v0.4.0 then refused it: "has schema 39, newer than this dockhand supports (32); use a newer build" (store/sqlite/sqlite.go), naming neither the build nor where to get it. What matters is whose database it is, not which command: a write command from that build would have locked v0.4.0 out the same, so migrating only on writes, as the notes suggest, moves the surprise rather than ending it. Where the newest session the database records (sessions' `version`, read before migrating as `refuseUnderLive` reads it) is a tagged release's and the build opening it isn't one, a development or rc build, it doesn't migrate unasked: on a terminal it asks, naming the release it locks out and the copy it keeps; without one it refuses, naming DOCKHAND_DB pointed at a copy to try the build. A release migrates as today, since that is the port's upgrade, serve's included. The newer schema's refusal names the build that last opened the database, and when, the way to that build (the port's upgrade for a release, its commit for a development build), and the copy kept at the older schema, with what was recorded since lost if it's put back. Done when a development build opening a database a release last opened asks on a terminal and refuses without one, a release build migrates it unasked, and an older build's refusal names the newer build and the copy. Not harm: nothing is lost, the copy is kept and said, and an older build refusing a newer schema is the designed refusal; v0.5.0 is a release, so its first command migrating a 0.4.0 database is the upgrade. v0.5.0 needs its notes alone: docs/releases/v0.5.0.md says the database migrates the first time v0.5.0 opens it, and adds that any command migrates it, `status` included, and that DOCKHAND_DB pointed at a copy tries a newer build without touching it.

- **Tart's capacity is said where it runs a check's environments in turn, and its doc agrees with it** (the exiv2 session's notes: at capacity 1 every environment ran in turn, a six-port check on four releases with its baseline taking hours, verified; under rc4 two VMs ran at once, inferred; about a tenth of a batch). The default has been 1 since 2026-10-02 (config.go `DefaultCapacity`), and `awaitSlot` was in rc4 too, so rc4's two VMs were likely two databases, whose leases can't see each other (inferred). docs/build-platforms.md says the capacity "defaults to two"; it says one, and that Apple's license allows two macOS guests, with `providers.tart.capacity = 2` to take them. A check's plan whose environments capacity 1 runs in turn says so once, with the key. The default stays 1, since it must suit the smallest Mac dockhand runs on. Done when the doc says one and a four-release plan at capacity 1 says it runs them in turn.

- **A link scan that couldn't read says why, once** (the exiv2 session's notes: `status -b` printed five "couldn't read what …'s build links" lines with no reason, verified; about a quarter batch). `LinkFinding.Unread` is a bool (view/stages.go `LinkWords`), so the line can't say whether the build failed, the result predates the scan, or the scan erred. The finding carries its reason, and status and the check's report fold the unread targets into one line with it, "couldn't read what 5 targets link: their builds failed", a line each only where the reasons differ. Done when the exiv2 case reads as one line with its reason.

- **The grid says that a cell from an earlier check stands** (the exiv2 session's notes: after a rebase the grid showed cells marked "(check-490)" with nothing saying whether they apply, verified; about a tenth of a batch). They apply: an earlier result fills a cell only where every read is unchanged, the target's directory, `_resources`, and every active port's directory at the same trees (reuse/current.go `Current`), across a rebase too, and the mark names the check that built it (evidence.go `CellCheck`, from the same session's notes of 2026-10-21). One line under a grid with such cells says so once: "(check-490): an earlier check's result, standing since nothing its build read has changed". Done when the exiv2 case's grid after its rebase carries the line, and a grid without such cells doesn't.

**Then v0.5.0, the final state** (two releases to it, the person's "Two releases", 2026-10-13; the order's history is in [the roadmap's history](activity/2026-10-16-roadmap-history.md#from-next-on-2026-10-17-v040s-parts-and-v050-as-it-stood)). After it, Then's items come at the person's word, and the release after v0.5.0 is v0.6.0, cut once Then's items 1 to 5 are done (the person's "After item 5", 2026-10-24 11:59Z, on the prioritization thread's card): it carries main's fixes placed above for the release after v0.5.0, the corpus's rest, and those five items, about 7.5 batches after v0.5.0's tag; items 6 to 15 follow it. A harm found in a release gets a point release on the quick stage alone, and field testing exercises main between tags.

- **v0.6.0 is cut once Then's items 1 to 5 are done** (the person's "After item 5", 2026-10-24 11:59Z, on the prioritization thread's card, over one release at Then's end or a point release of the fixes first): it carries main's fixes for the release after v0.5.0, nine on 2026-10-24 with six more placed that day from two sessions' notes, the corpus's rest, and the typed source, per-port holds, `update --hand-edit`, the baseline on every check, and the GitHub allowance, about 7.5 batches after v0.5.0's tag. A point release stays for harm alone.

From the exiv2 session's notes (2026-10-24, [note](activity/2026-10-24-field-notes-coalesced.md)):

- **A failed check's guest, kept for a shell** (its rc4 notes, holding on rc5). After a failure, the VM is kept, stopped, for `dockhand shell check-N <release>` to start and enter at the port's work directory, deleted at the branch's next check or by `clean`; a VM's disk is the cost. About 1.5 batches (inferred).

- **ports.macports.org's build history in `impact`.** Whether a port built on each release on MacPorts' builders before, to choose between a patch and `known_fail`, the site's API read once a port. About half a batch.

Found after rc3, while checking Codex's review against main (a268b6e5), fixed on main, and in rc4 (the person's "Go ahead and tag an rc4", 2026-10-17 14:43Z):

A person's own `ship <port>` on a terminal still keeps one, for the edit by hand. Done when a `ship --mine` over a port dockhand can't edit leaves no `dockhand/*` branch. Done 2026-10-17 ([note](activity/2026-10-17-after-rc3.md)).
- **A hand edit `ship` holds names the way on.** Under `ship --yes`, the hold says tidy's "apply it as shown with --yes", which ship's `--yes` doesn't do; it names `dockhand tidy --branch <name>`, as serve's hold does. Done 2026-10-17 ([note](activity/2026-10-17-after-rc3.md)).
- **`dockhand setup` counts a golden copy.** With a release's base image deleted and its golden copy kept, setup says "not set up" and offers to download macOS, up to 60 GB (buildenv/tart/setup.go `Status`, command/providers.go, command/setup.go), where `setup tart` restores it in about a minute and check says so (D-R3). `Status` records the releases it can restore, and the line and the offer say the restore. Done 2026-10-17 ([note](activity/2026-10-17-after-rc3.md)).

Found by field testing at 5897a828, after rc5 was tagged on it, each a fix for main that ships in v0.5.0 and is v0.4.0's known issue until then, since no rc6 is cut (the person's "No rc6", 2026-10-19 19:40Z):
- **A license file whose licenses are unchanged says what it adds beside them.** libxkbcommon 1.7.0 to 1.13.2 held, "upstream's LICENSE was MIT and HPND and HPND-sell-variant by its text, and now reads as no license that's known" (macports/macports-ports#35691, submitted anyway). The same three licenses are found at both ends. Labels and copyright lines no license's match takes in no longer count against coverage (5b3a949d), but 1.13.2 still reads 71.0%, under `wholeText`'s 75. What's left is real: the statements for its test data add Anton Zinoviev's terms ("This software may be used, modified, copied, distributed, and sold…"), 241 words 1.7.0 hasn't, which no license dockhand knows matches. So it holds, rightly. A section for files that aren't installed isn't left out, since a LICENSE's headings are its own prose and don't say what a port installs. The wording is wrong: the file doesn't read as no license, it reads as the same three with terms beside them. Where the licenses found are the old ones and only the coverage fell, the hold says so and quotes the added terms' first line where it can find it (sourcecompare/compare.go `licenseWords`). Done when 1.13.2's hold names the three licenses and quotes "This software may be used, modified, copied, distributed, and sold". Done 2026-10-19 (5b3a949d, 0680b7f8).
- **A library a configure.ac names through a variable isn't removed.** GraphicsMagick 1.3.45 to 1.3.49 held on about twenty libraries "removed", brotlidec, freetype, and jasper to zstd, with "pkg-config module libturbojpeg removed" (macports/macports-ports#35698, submitted after a look). 1.3.49 checks them as `AC_CHECK_LIB([$zstd], …)`, with `zstd=zstd` set above and replaced by pkg-config's `-l` where it answers. The reader skips a name a macro computes (project/autoconf.go), so it reads 24 libraries in 1.3.49 where it reads 43 in 1.3.45, and the comparison takes each one missing as removed (dockhand's reader run on the two releases' configure.ac). A `$name` set to a literal word in the file reads as that library; one it can't resolve, as 1.3.49's `$pnglibbase`, leaves the list incomplete, and nothing missing from an incomplete list is said removed. Read that way, only brotlidec, which 1.3.49 links only beside libjxl, and the png names leave. The libturbojpeg module is right: 1.3.49 checks libjpeg instead. Done when 1.3.45 to 1.3.49 says neither freetype nor zstd removed, and still says the module. Done 2026-10-19 (0d4f4fe1): 1.3.45 to 1.3.49 now says only `--with-urwbase35otf-font-dir` and libsharpyuv added and the libturbojpeg module removed.

Tidy takes in an untracked file a changed port's `patchfiles` names, with the commit that names it, as `git add` would, an ignored one said rather than taken, and lists any other untracked file in a changed port's directory as left out, so it never says nothing is uncommitted while one is there; it doesn't take in the rest, which may be a `.rej` or a build's leavings. The check's plan, in the engine, so check, ship, and serve all meet it before any VM starts, refuses a changed port whose `patchfiles` names a file the capture doesn't hold, "net-snmp's patchfiles names files/patch-swinst_darwin-declaration.diff, which isn't tracked: git add it, or check --include it", a hold where nobody looks; item 7's one pipeline then carries it. A tracked `git rm` of a patch, as e2fsprogs', works already. Done 2026-10-20 (8424f033): the branch as it was tidies to the fixed branch's tree, patch included, and `check` refuses it untracked; `ship` holds for the tidy plan, which takes the patch in, so its check never runs without it.

In a branch's worktree, it says the branch has no check yet, and `dockhand check` runs one; outside one, the hint stays. Done when the A10 row reads it. - **status's "Needs you" fits the terminal** (the gnome-voice session's notes, 2026-10-20, and field testing's own, [note](activity/2026-10-20-gnome-voice-field-notes.md)). `writeAttention` puts each row's message and its command in one tabwriter's columns (command/status.go), so one long message, a stale check's, sets every row's width, and the commands start near column 400. A row's command goes on the line below its message, indented, so no row's width depends on another's; a message wraps at the terminal's width, 80 where it's unknown. - **A guest is offered the kept archives of what this revision needs, and says what it took.** dockhand keeps every archive a guest installed a dependency from, MacPorts' own included (engine/archives.go `KeepDependency`), and offers a later guest those of its targets' direct dependencies and of every port an earlier result of a target had active (engine/reuse.go `dependencyInstalls`). The offer is the dependency closure of what the guest builds, at the check's revision, from the graph the plan reads (`DependencyGraph`, plan.go), never an earlier result's active ports, so a port the new version dropped isn't copied into the guest and a dependency's dependency is offered on the first check too. The line before the build says how many are offered, "the guest may install up to N dependencies from archives earlier checks kept", and the target's result names those it installed from them, by what the guest reports it consumed. - **A pull request's Description leaves out attribution trailers, and a note that is all of it is said plainly** (the gnome-voice session's submission, 2026-10-20; field testing's #35685, #35709, #35710, and #35712). With one commit whose body is only `Assisted-by: …`, the Description was that line alone, which `submit --plan` warned of (prdescription.go `SaysNothing`), and a `--note` is always a quote led by "**Author's note:**" (`noteWords`), which reads as someone else's words where it's the whole Description. The commit body's attribution trailers, `Assisted-by`, `Co-authored-by`, and `Signed-off-by` beside dockhand's own `Generated-By`, which `commitBody` alone leaves out today, aren't put in the Description; a reference trailer, as MacPorts' `Closes: https://trac.macports.org/ticket/N`, stays, since it tells a reviewer the ticket. A note where nothing else is said, no new port and a single commit whose body is then empty, is the Description itself, unquoted, a line of it that would begin a heading escaped so the Description still ends where dockhand reads it to (`sectionSpan`); beside other text it stays the quote. Done when a one-commit submission whose body is only `Assisted-by` and a `--note` gives the note alone as its Description, and `submit --pr` reads it back as its own. Open pull requests keep their text unless submitted again. Done 2026-10-20 (9b8038d4).
- **A table of a branch's releases shows those its checks planned** (the gnome-voice session's notes, 2026-10-20: "macOS 26 · not run" for a branch only ever checked on 12 and 27, from a Mac on 26). The evidence adds build.default's required releases for submit to say which no check covered, and status's branch table showed them as columns; it shows `Evidence.Checked()`, those a check planned, as the matrix leaves out a release a profile skipped (0cf27aaa). check's own report does too: a check `--on 12,27` is about 12 and 27, and what build.default still wants is for submit's line on the releases no check covered to say, not a column of its report. A branch's evidence is judged against every environment a check of its files planned, beside build.default's (evidence.Plan, since sand-runner's check on 15 alone hid its pass on 26), and a target a check's `--only` left out is a cell not run in each of that check's environments (evidence.Judge), so it's missing there: one diagnostic `check --only ufraw` on 13 and 14 made `submit --plan` ask for all six of the branch's ports there. Where build.default's profile doesn't cover an environment (`Covers`), a target is required there only where a check of the files planned it; the others' cells there read "—", as a release a profile skipped does, and count for nothing. What a check found there still counts, a failure included, and where a target a check planned there is still unbuilt, as when the check stopped before it, submit names that check. Not taken: pinning or narrowing the required set for a branch, since the profile is what submission needs (D17), and once only what a check planned binds, a narrow check raises nothing to narrow. Done when that branch, its six ports passed on build.default's releases and ufraw checked `--only` on 13 and 14, submits asking nothing more of the other five, and a port a check planned on 15 and stopped before is still asked for there.
