- **The `body-wrap` rule flags a long line beside a URL** (the same notes, note 9). The exiv2 session's `submit --plan` flagged ufraw's commit and not a 79-character body line in libextractor's, an earlier commit, which read as the tip alone checked. Every commit is read (`CheckCommits`, commitrules/rules.go, for submit, tidy, and review alike), so the likely cause, inferred, is the exemption: a line over 72 characters is skipped wherever it holds "://", where the rule as explain.go words it spares only a line that is only a URL. It spares that line, and a trailer whose value is a URL, as `Closes: https://trac.macports.org/ticket/N`, and nothing else. To reproduce first, on libextractor's commit as it was; if its line held no URL, the cause is found there. Done when that commit's line is flagged, with ufraw's. No known-issues row: a warning missed, which MacPorts' reviewers read for too. libextractor's commit as it stands (72e32cd7) has no line over 61 characters, so it couldn't be reproduced from it; a synthetic 79-character line holding a URL was, and is flagged now. Done 2026-10-21 (83450301, 5ed1c70e).
- **The upstream comparison reads the build files where the port's build reads them** (field testing at c643559a, 2026-10-21). `update i2pd` from 2.61.0 to 3.0.0 said "no license, build file, or dependency changes", having compared LICENSE and debian/copyright alone: the port sets `cmake.source_dir ${worksrcpath}/build`, and build/CMakeLists.txt grew from 418 to 465 lines with what the Portfile had to follow, a C++23/20 probe where C++17 was set (field testing raised `compiler.cxx_standard` to 2020), `option(WITH_TORRENTS … ON)`, and Boost's `OPTIONAL_COMPONENTS json url`. A reading takes build files and manifests from the project's root alone, worksrcdir's subdirectory (project/read.go `wanted`, macports/buildsystems.go `SourceSubdirectory`), and the evaluator reports none of the options that move a build below it (macports/options.go `ReadOptions`). The root's build files are read, and with them those of each directory below worksrcpath that the port's build systems (`PortInfo.BuildSystems`) read theirs from: `cmake.source_dir` for CMake, `configure.dir` for Autotools and Meson, whose source directory it is, `cargo.dir` for Cargo, and `build.dir` for Go, Python, and Perl. In the tree at ad354b7f, 25 Portfiles move `cmake.source_dir` below worksrcpath, 56 others `configure.dir`, and about 45 Go, Cargo, Python, and Perl ones `build.dir`; no Meson port moves `configure.dir` today. The evaluator reports the four options, the reading cache's version is bumped, and so is the assessment policy's, so a stale "no changes" is collected again. Replayed on the set, i2pd, and a port for each option; no whole-tree pair, since the evaluator reads four more options as it reads `go.vendors` where a port doesn't set it, and evaluates nothing new. Not taken here: reading the C++ standard a CMakeLists.txt asks for, which i2pd 3.0.0 probes for, so its file reads as changed and the diff says the rest. Done when i2pd 2.61.0 to 3.0.0 says build/CMakeLists.txt changed, naming WITH_TORRENTS added. Known-issues row: for a port that sets one of these below its source, `dockhand diff --source --stat` lists that directory's build file; read its change before submitting. Done 2026-10-21 (2d7c33ec): i2pd says build/CMakeLists.txt changed, WITH_TORRENTS added.
- **`update --follow-moves` follows a move on a branch already at the version** (field testing's duckdb 1.5.6 to 2.0.0, at 9bdc25ca, 2026-10-21). `update duckdb` said "upstream transferred from cwida to duckdb … update --follow-moves points github.setup at it"; `dockhand update duckdb --follow-moves` in that branch then changed nothing, and the Portfile kept `github.setup cwida duckdb 2.0.0 v`. The move is looked for, and followed, only where the update changes files (engine/update.go: `renamed` and `MoveGitHub` sit under `len(result.Files) > 0`), and a branch already at 2.0.0 has none to change, which field testing guessed and the code shows. With `--follow-moves`, an update that finds the port at its release still asks GitHub for the repository and points github.setup at the move, as the edit, said "Updated github.setup to duckdb/duckdb"; the comparison's moved finding then reads as followed, so its hold goes. The hint stays as it is, since it's then true wherever it's given. Done when duckdb's branch at 2.0.0 reads `github.setup duckdb duckdb 2.0.0 v` after that command, and its submit plan holds no longer on the transfer. Known-issues row: pass `--follow-moves` on the update that moves the version, or edit github.setup's owner by hand. Done 2026-10-21 (165dee02): replayed in scratch, the branch reads `github.setup duckdb duckdb 2.0.0 v`, and its submit plan holds no longer on the transfer.
- **A build option the Portfile sets that upstream removed is said first** (the same bump). duckdb 2.0.0's CMakeLists.txt drops ENABLE_EXTENSION_AUTOLOADING and ENABLE_EXTENSION_AUTOINSTALL, which the Portfile passes as `-D…=1`, and the comparison said "option COVERAGE added … and 7 more": `cmakeWords` (sourcecompare/compare.go) lists added options, then defaults moved, then removed, and names `cmakeNamed`, 5, so the two fell into the count. CMake only warns of a variable no file uses, so the build goes on without what the Portfile asked. What the Portfile names, by `named` (assess.go, `portfile.Mentions`), leads and is never counted away: "option ENABLE_EXTENSION_AUTOLOADING, which the Portfile sets, was removed", and a default it relies on that moves is said the same way; the rest keep their order and count. Autotools' `--enable-` and `--with-` lists, compared in the same file, do the same. Where no Portfile was read, `named` answers yes to everything, so nothing is said to be the Portfile's there. Done when duckdb 1.5.6 to 2.0.0 names both options as the Portfile's, before the added ones. No known-issues row: the file's change is said, and `dockhand diff --source` shows the rest. Done 2026-10-21 (ee011731): duckdb 1.5.6 to 2.0.0 names both as the Portfile's, before the added ones.
- **An added option says what it gates** (the same bump, 2026-10-21). duckdb 2.0.0's comparison said "option LINK_CORE_EXTENSIONS added, off by default", but 2.0.0 builds the extensions BUILD_EXTENSIONS names, as the Portfile sets them, and links them only with that option on, so the default build no longer links autocomplete and ducklake ("-- Extensions built but not linked: [autocomplete, ducklake]" in the configure log); only the port's test phase caught it. Reproduced on the two releases' sources: the gate is in extension/extension_build_tools.cmake, which the CMakeLists.txt `include()`s and the comparison reads, as `if(LINK_CORE_EXTENSIONS) duckdb_extension_statically_link(${EXT}) endif()` in 2.0.0, where in 1.5.6 linking was `register_extension`'s default unless `DONT_LINK` was passed. No command the default build reached is gone, so a rule for what it stops reaching wouldn't fire, and telling that a function's behaviour moved under an option takes reading what the function does, which nothing generic can. duckdb holds already, on the CMakeLists.txt's other changes; what was missing is what the option gates. An added option's line names the commands its `if()` blocks gate, beside `set()`, `message()`, and the `if()`s, by name and file, the first two and a count: "option LINK_CORE_EXTENSIONS added, off by default, gating duckdb_extension_statically_link in extension/extension_build_tools.cmake". It claims nothing about what the build lost, and holds nothing D12 doesn't. Where a gated command sits in an `if()` or `foreach()` that names a variable the Portfile sets (`named`), the line leads as the Portfile's, as a removed option's does (ee011731), "…, inside a foreach() over BUILD_EXTENSIONS, which the Portfile sets"; `foreach()` joins the blocks the reading records, as `if()` is (`CMakeStatement.Under`). Not taken: reading a configure log's `-- ` status lines for words like "not linked" or "skipped", as field testing suggested, since each project words its own, and a configure prints hundreds. Done when duckdb 1.5.6 to 2.0.0 names duckdb_extension_statically_link as LINK_CORE_EXTENSIONS' gate, leading where its loop names BUILD_EXTENSIONS, and fluent-bit 5.1.3's FLB_PROTOBUF_ENCODER still reads with its `find_package(Protobuf)`. No known-issues row: the build file's change holds and is said. Done 2026-10-21 (20f0cf8d): duckdb leads with LINK_CORE_EXTENSIONS' gate, its loop naming BUILD_EXTENSIONS, and fluent-bit 5.1.3 still reads find_package(Protobuf); the loops are a field of their own, not Under, whose words say if()s.
- **setup's Tart line is a line for each release, and says what checks build in** (the person, 2026-10-21 21:50Z: "Can we have a more comprehensive listing for the `tart` output under `dockhand setup`? It's just one big run-on", with a bullet for each macOS version, its remedy indented below, "or let me know if you have a better idea"). On his Mac it read "✓ images for macOS 12, 13, 14, 15, 26, 27, with Xcode for 12, 13, 14, 15, 26, 27; macOS 12's image, macOS 13's image, … have MacPorts 2.12.6, older than this Mac's 2.12.7: dockhand setup tart monterey --rebuild; …" in one line (command/providers.go `tartReadiness`, through `OlderMacPorts`). Those six are the base images, and none is what a check builds in: where a release has an Xcode image, every check there builds in it (buildenv/tart/provider.go, `DeveloperToolsXcode` wherever `xcodeImage` exists), and an image made again installs its own MacPorts (setup.go `newMacPorts`), so the Xcode images, on 2.12.7, weren't behind, and the line asked for six rebuilds no check needed. The line becomes a heading, "✓ ready for macOS 12, 13, 14, 15, 26, and 27", and under it a line for each release, aligned: its name, the image its checks build in, and that image's MacPorts, "macOS 12 Monterey   Xcode image   MacPorts 2.12.7" (`Status` records each image's MacPorts from its manifest, where it records only those behind today). Where the image checks build in is behind this Mac's MacPorts, its line says so, "MacPorts 2.12.6, older than this Mac's 2.12.7", with `RemakeWords`' rebuild indented under it; where only the base images under Xcode images are behind, one line after the releases says it, "Their base images have MacPorts 2.12.6, which no check builds with while the release has its Xcode image", with no rebuild listed, since `setup tart <release>` says its own. A release with no image, one to restore, or no image for this Mac's release keeps its words, on its own line. Done when the person's Mac reads six aligned release lines and the one base-image line, no line wider than the terminal, and a release whose Xcode image is behind shows its rebuild under it. No known-issues row: it's layout. Done 2026-10-21 ([note](activity/2026-10-21-tart-line.md)), tested with the six-release case; the person's Mac reads it at the next `dockhand setup`.
- **A plan says which subports a branch changes, and its exclusions a line per reason** (field testing's terraform replay at f6d5281c, 2026-10-22, notes 1 and 3: branch terraform-1.16-1.16.7, whose diff is inside `subport terraform-1.16 {` alone). `check --plan` said "Changed 19 ports" (terraform_select, terraform-1.0 … and 11 more), which field testing read, as it did in batches 40 and 47, as the changed set being too broad. The 19 are as decided: a check builds every subport of a changed Portfile, as MacPorts' CI does (the person's "Agreed, we'll follow MacPorts CI", kept in [the roadmap's history](activity/2026-10-16-roadmap-history.md)), and `--only` narrows by hand. What misleads is the label: `WritePlan` (command/view/writers.go) calls every target of a changed directory "Changed", where the revision's change record knows only terraform-1.16 changed, and status already says so (`recordedPorts`). Where a directory's record narrows it (`changedSubports`), the plan's Changed line names those subports and the rest follow on a line of their own, "Siblings    terraform_select, terraform-1.0 … and 10 more, built as MacPorts' CI builds every subport of a changed Portfile; --only narrows"; a directory with no record, or one that keeps its text scope, reads as today, and the JSON's roles stay. The same plan printed 25 "Excluded" lines, a port each, since `GroupedExclusions` (model/plan.go) groups by port and reason; they group by reason and releases across ports, the ports named as the Changed line names them, "Excluded    terraform_select, terraform-1.0 … and 11 more on macOS 13, 14, 15, and 26: minmax builds each on its oldest and newest releases, macOS 12 and 27", the reason's words reading for one port or many. Done when terraform-1.16's plan names it alone as changed, its siblings on their own line, and an Excluded line for each distinct reason and set of releases. No known-issues row: it's wording. Done 2026-10-22 (39d24f17, d97b03ad, [note](activity/2026-10-22-terraform-replay.md)).
- **A subport's result stands across an edit inside another subport's block** (the same replay, note 2). check-550 reused all of check-549, on the same commit. On a new commit no subport's result would be, so the replay field testing offered, master's terraform checked and then the branch, isn't needed to know it: an earlier result stands only where the target's directory has the same tree (reuse/current.go `Current`), and any edit to terraform's Portfile changes the tree for every subport. So each patch bump of terraform-1.16 builds its 18 siblings again on every release a check plans, though none runs a line that changed: MacPorts runs a `subport X { … }` block's body only when evaluating X. The rule widens by that alone. Where a target's directory differs from its earlier build's only in Portfile lines inside the literal blocks of other subports (the attribution `portfile.UnseenChanges` makes already: "a change inside a subport's literal block is that subport's; one anywhere else is every subport's"), and its other files, `_resources`, and each active port's directory are as recorded (an active port in the same directory, as terraform_select may be, by the same rule), the earlier result stands, shown as "✓ (check-N)". A Portfile that doesn't parse on either side, a change outside every block, or an earlier tree the repository no longer holds reuses nothing, as today. The earlier Portfile is read from the recorded tree, so nothing migrates. Coverage stays the person's (every subport of a changed Portfile has a result, as CI builds them), and only the subport an edit can reach is built. Done when field testing's replay, master's terraform checked and then the branch, builds terraform-1.16 alone on the branch and reads its siblings as reused from master's check. No known-issues row: today's rule builds more, never claims more. Done 2026-10-22 (1f445dc7, [note](activity/2026-10-22-terraform-replay.md)): the replay's check-553 built terraform-1.16 and reused its siblings; terraform_select built beside it only because the replay's copied database had no archives folder, so a replay copies that folder with it.
- **`bump` and `clean` take `--plan`; `create` and `rebase` are named as having none** (part 5's item 6, 047f29ce, which left the four without it, 2026-10-22). §9 asks `--plan` of every command that changes something. `bump <port> --plan` shows what `ship <port>` would do, ship's own preview, since bump is ship's unattended front for one port; `clean --plan` prints the plan clean already shows before it asks (`PlanClean`, `PlanCleanBranches`) and removes nothing, as a run without `--yes` off a terminal does today. Both are a few lines on paths that exist. `create` and `rebase` would need plans of their own, and each is undone already (an archived branch; rebase's checkpoint and `undo`), so they go to Later, and the contract test names them as the two without one, so a new command can't join them unseen. Done when `bump jq --plan` and `clean --plan` change nothing and print what a run would do, and the contract test's list of commands without `--plan` is exactly create and rebase. No known-issues row. Done 2026-10-22: the contract test's `noPlan` names every command without `--plan`, each with why, create and rebase as Later's.

Before v0.5.0's acceptance, the open known issues of v0.4.0 that are cheap to close, about 1.5 batches, each a fix for main whose README row goes with it (the person's "Is there anything else you think that could be jammed into v0.5.0?", 2026-10-22 21:33Z, with Then's items kept out, since each adds findings or a migration that acceptance would then have to prove; taken at the person's "Fix them", 2026-10-22 21:43Z). The rows that stay say what dockhand chose or can't know: `build.default`'s `host`, GitHub's hourly allowance, a new macOS release's Rust and Go toolchains, and serve's one doubled line, which hasn't recurred. py-lmdb's row is checked by acceptance's replay, which holds py-lmdb, now that strict fidelity observes what an edit changes; it goes if the replay reads it as planned. It went (1ecbdc53): py-lmdb planned soundly on rc3 to rc5, and acceptance's C6 expects a plan.
- **A Go module that moved hosts is said.** pomo moved from GitHub to Codeberg, and its update was planned without naming the move; the row asks a person to compare go.mod's module path with the Portfile's. The comparison reads each side's go.mod already (project/gomod.go, `GoModule.Path`). Where the new release's module path differs from the old one's, it's an upstream finding held for a look, naming both, "go.mod's module moved from github.com/kevinschoon/pomo to codeberg.org/kevinschoon/pomo; the Portfile's go.setup names the old path". Done when a fixture whose go.mod's module moves holds and names both paths. Done 2026-10-22 (b82e4367, [note](activity/2026-10-22-last-fixes.md)).
- **`checksums` says a hand-edited version left its vendored lists behind.** A Rust or Go port whose version a person edited by hand keeps its old `cargo.crates` or `go.vendors`, since `checksums` refreshes only the port's own archives, and nothing says so. Where the version differs from the base's and the list didn't change, `checksums` changes nothing and says which list is the old version's, with the way on: undo the version edit, then `dockhand update <port> <version>`, which regenerates it. Done when a Rust fixture with its version edited by hand gets that, and its Portfile is left as it was. Done 2026-10-22 (b82e4367, the rule tested on its own, [note](activity/2026-10-22-last-fixes.md)).
- **A refused fetch is tried again a day later.** A fetch during `update` that a server refuses, as a 404 does, holds the branch until a file in it is edited, though the archive may be served again, where an outage or a rate limit is fetched again at the next look (engine/update.go, `PreviousProblem`, the architecture review's L2b). A refusal is fetched again at the first look a day after it was recorded, and holds until then. Done when a branch held on a 404 is released at the first look a day later at which the archive is served. Done 2026-10-22 (407a1aeb, [note](activity/2026-10-22-last-fixes.md)).
- **A patch changed under its own name is said.** A revision that changes only a patch's contents, keeping its name, reads as fetching the base's source, so the assessment compares nothing for it and says nothing of the patch. The submit plan names each patch whose contents changed, "files/patch-foo.diff changed; dockhand diff shows it", a notice holding nothing, since a check applies it and fails where it doesn't apply. Done when such a revision's submit plan names its patch. Done 2026-10-22 (407a1aeb, [note](activity/2026-10-22-last-fixes.md)).
- **`create` exits non-zero where a Go draft has no checksums.** For a Go module older than go 1.17, whose go.sum names modules its go.mod doesn't, create writes a draft with zero checksums and exits 0, its JSON's `checksums_problem` naming the rows to add. It exits non-zero, as a draft that isn't done, and prints the rows to add to `go.vendors` with their locks and `port -v checksum` as the way to their checksums. Done when such a create exits non-zero and the terminal shows the rows. Done 2026-10-22 (407a1aeb; it exits 3, for a look, [note](activity/2026-10-22-last-fixes.md)).

Found by Codex's architecture review of rc1's 8c007979 ([review](reviews/2026-10-22-architecture-8c007979.md), with its probes; C8-1 to C8-10), assessed by the architecture thread on 2026-10-23 at main cfa4530f, rc2's commit and the review's, with all ten holding and its four probes failing there ([coalescing note](activity/2026-10-23-codex-review-8c007979-coalesced.md)). Three are fixes for main that rc3 carries before the M1's full stage, about two batches, each probe becoming a test at its owner, at the person's "Let's do an rc3" (2026-10-23 00:23Z), with C8-5's serve hold beside them; rc2's quick stage runs on meanwhile, and its status, ship, serve, and watch rows are the ones rc3 repeats. The rule for blocking is the release bar's: v0.5.0 would act on the wrong object, or against a decision the person made. Tagging rc2 with these as known issues would ship two of v0.5.0's own features misbehaving in the release meant as the final state.
- **A command typed at `status --watch` runs where the watch does** (C8-1, P1). root.go installs the process's `Run` as watch's nested runner, which reads `os.Getwd` and `os.Environ`, and the prompt's line carries the verb and the branch alone (status/watch.go `watchVerb`), so a watch started with `--tree`, `--db`, or `--git` runs `l`, `c`, or `s` against the process's directory and the default database: the review's probe runs `logs check-1` in a directory that isn't a ports tree. New with part 4.2's split. The nested runner is `RunWith` bound to its own invocation (the line's environment and directory, the same dependencies and host), the nested line carries the persistent flags in effect ahead of the typed verb, and the line printed stays the one typed. Done when command/status's test, two trees and two databases holding the same branch and check names, shows the nested command ran in the watch's own (about half a batch).
- **A standing acceptance reaches every reader of readiness** (C8-3). `PlanSubmit` reads the branch's decisions and the standing ones (engine/submit.go) and passes, but `Facts` (engine/status.go) carries none, and branchstate's `SubmitReadiness` needs a passed check, so `PassingBranches` (serve's candidates and `ship --passing`), the attention rows, and `Next:` judge without them: the probe's standing libgcc15 acceptance gives submit no blocks and serve no candidate, and status keeps asking about a failure the person accepted. The ledger note says bump and serve take a standing acceptance, and the case is the person's libgcc15 decision of 2026-10-18. One eligibility, computed once: `BranchStatus` reads the accepted ports and standing decisions beside the evidence, `Facts` carries them, and branchstate says once whether evidence with decisions leaves a publication block (the `PublicationBlocks` submit calls), which `SubmitReadiness`, attention, `Next:`, and `PlanSubmit` all read; the run's outcome stays failed. Done when one agreement test gives the same evidence and decisions through status, submit, `ship --passing`, and serve's candidates, and the acceptance lapses there when the dependency's version or the image changes (about one batch).
- **A link is judged in the environment that built it** (C8-2, riding rc3). engine/links.go keys a target's declared libraries by the target alone across environments, so a `depends_lib` only the x86_64 evaluation declares hides the arm64 build's undeclared link (the probe's oniguruma), the case part 6's notice is for. The rule takes each build with its result, environment, and that environment's declarations, judges each, and merges equal findings for display, keeping where the declaration is missing; "read in any environment counts" stays the separate policy it is. Done when evidence's test, the probe, reads the arm64 link as undeclared (about half a batch).
- **An unknown current commit holds serve** (C8-5's gap, the person's "I agree that we should hold in that instance", 2026-10-23 00:23Z). `movedSources` (engine/servesubmit.go) skips a source whose current commit couldn't be read, so serve submitted with its freshness unknown, where branchstate's note says unknown freshness holds unattended publication. Unknown holds serve, as an unread archive does, and an attended submit says it and goes on; a network blip costs serve a hold its next pass clears. The contract test asks it. Done when serve holds a branch whose source's current commit couldn't be read, and `submit --plan` says the freshness is unknown.
- **docs/architecture.md says X1 is done**, with rc3: it still says composition moves in future and that the engine opens the store itself.

Found by field testing after rc3 was tagged on 25f16ace, each a fix for main that rides the next tag and never gets one of its own: rc4 took the first; rc5, cut for the harm below, takes those done before its tag, the amend rule aside; the rest ride the release after v0.5.0. Only a harm by the release bar, as rc1's Rust refusal was, asks for a tag:
- **A clean link scan says it ran** (field testing's crystal 1.22.1, the first real run of part 6's scan, 2026-10-23). Where every library a check's builds link is declared, the check's report and status's Links row print nothing (view/stages.go `WriteLinks`, status.go's Links row), which reads as a scan that never ran. Where the scan read a target's links and found each declared, one line says it, "Links    crystal links 5 ports, each declared in depends_lib", in the check's report and on status's row, its count the ports the links map to; a target the scan couldn't read, as a failed build's, says nothing, as today. Not Then, as implementation suggested: it's a line of part 6's own output, a quarter batch, and Then's items are features at the person's word. Done when crystal's check and status each print the line, within the width, said once. No known-issues row. Done 2026-10-23 (4aff01ca), in rc4.
- **Every port that lists `go.vendors` updates again** (field testing's git-credential-oauth 0.18.0, 2026-10-23; rc5's, at the person's "Cut rc5", 12:17Z). `update` refused git-credential-oauth for two of v0.5.0's own changes, neither in v0.4.0, and both meet every port that lists `go.vendors`, since golang 1.0 handles each list the same way: 179 Portfiles, 176 of them on GitHub (counted in a ports tree of 2026-10-02, and confirmed by implementation's replays on rc4 and main, mailpit's, git-credential-oauth's, and direnv's; each Go port field testing updated since 2026-10-19 builds with `go.offline_build no`, so none met it). First, golang 1.0's `checksums-append ${distfile}` (golang-1.0.tcl:521) names a vendored archive alone, its sums following on a declaration of their own, and the PortGroup refusal (`portGroupChecksums`, portedit/dependencies.go, from 2d61c8c3, narrowed for cargo in 32427a61) took a declaration carrying no sum as the PortGroup's; one carrying no value now counts as the Portfile's. Second, `go.vendors` sets `extract.rename no` and wraps `extract.cmd` (golang-1.0.tcl:427 to 431), which strict fidelity's block step (6367e40b) didn't predict, the mistake rc1 made on Rust's `dist_subdir` (1b3899e7); portedit's blockSettings table now predicts what each block's PortGroup sets. Fixed on main by implementation, with two tests (52d14a6c). It blocks v0.5.0 by the release bar: Go updates on GitHub are the release's scope, v0.4.0 planned these ports, and rc1's refusal of every cargo port blocked alike. Since the table sits in the block step every update's edit goes through, rc5's M1 run repeats the rows that plan an update, and the rest of rc4's results carry over. Done 2026-10-23 on main (52d14a6c): mailpit 1.31.4 to 1.32.0, a block of 275 lines, and git-credential-oauth 0.18.0 plan, where rc4 refuses them. Before rc5's tag the quick stage gains its row, C11, git-credential-oauth's, the field's case, at pin 026b3878, as dust, pgdog, and doge are Rust's.
- **A Go module's major-version suffix isn't a move** (implementation's replays for rc5, 2026-10-23; before rc5's tag, about a quarter batch). direnv's update refuses, "dependency: go.mod's module is github.com/direnv/direnv/v2, where go.package is github.com/direnv/direnv" (depblock/go.go, `generateGo`), since the comparison takes Go's `/vN` suffix as part of the path, and the old version's check says the module "moved" (portedit/dependencies.go). Older than v0.4.0 (173967dc, 2026-10-02), it was hidden in rc1 to rc4 behind the checksum refusal above, and meets every port that lists `go.vendors` whose module is at major version 2 or above. The comparison takes each path without its major-version suffix (golang.org/x/mod/module's `SplitPathVersion`, already a dependency, which reads gopkg.in's `.vN` too). The assessment's `moduleMoved` (assess/toolchain.go) keeps comparing whole paths, so a release that moves its module to a new major version still holds for a look. Done when direnv plans on main and the quick stage gains its row. Done 2026-10-23 (8f8a3919): direnv 2.38.2 plans, and the quick stage's C12 reads it.
- **An evaluation's reply fits the RPC frame however long a port's lists** (implementation's replays for rc5, 2026-10-23; before rc5's tag if it's within half a batch). opkssh 0.17.0 to 0.19.1 refuses on rc4 and main alike, "baseline revision evaluation was inconclusive: … rpc: frame limit exceeded: frame advertises 18529452 bytes, limit is 16777216" (tcl/rpc/session.go, `defaultFrameLimit`, 16 MiB since 2026-09-15), its `go.vendors` about 500 lines, where mailpit's 275 fit. Of the `go.vendors` blocks in a ports tree of 2026-10-02, eight run past 430 lines and five past 500, caddy's to 881. A reply that size for 500 lines suggests each `checksums-append`, `distfiles-append`, and `master_sites-append` the PortGroup makes is recorded with the option's whole value so far, which grows with the square of the list (inferred, not measured). Implementation measures what the frame carries first: where a declaration carries the accumulated value, it carries its own call's words instead, and the limit stays; raising the limit alone moves the cliff to the next longer list. Whether v0.4.0 plans opkssh is untested; it refuses with a reason, so it's no harm by the release bar. Done when opkssh and caddy plan on main and the quick stage gains opkssh's row. Bigger than half a batch, it rides the release after v0.5.0 instead of holding rc5, with a known-issues row naming the longest lists' ports. Done 2026-10-23 (8f8a3919), measured first, the cause not the one inferred: each recorded declaration carried its stack frames, and each `checksums-append` had the whole `go.vendors` command on its stack, n copies of an n-row command (a 600-row live test sent 34 MB). observation.tcl sends each frame once, in a table its events name by index, and the 16 MiB limit stays. opkssh 0.19.1 plans, and the quick stage's C13 reads it; caddy's reply now fits, and its update refuses by the designed rule for a patch that edits dependency manifests, which meets the line, since the frame was what caddy tested.

v0.5.0's release, once rc5's acceptance passes, as v0.4.0's went (2026-10-21), each step on main in docs alone, so the tag stays on the accepted candidate's commit:
- **The README's known issues become v0.5.0's.** "Known issues in v0.4.0" gives way to "Known issues in v0.5.0", each row read again against the accepted commit, with its workaround, and marked fixed on `main` where it is. The v0.4.0 rows that stay: `build.default`'s `host`, GitHub's hourly allowance (its row adding that signed out the limit is 60 requests an hour, which serve's watch of one open pull request spends, and to sign in with `dockhand setup github`), a new macOS release's Rust and Go toolchains, and serve's doubled line if it has recurred; py-lmdb's went, read as planned on rc3 to rc5 (1ecbdc53). Those placed above for after v0.5.0: after you pull a collaborator's commit and discard it with a reset, `submit` replaces it, its preview saying so (the amend rule); and a port whose project moved to a new repository, its tags stopping below the port's version, reads as current, as mergestat, carl, and zed with zq do (fixed on `main`, 1c9d5a47). Every other v0.4.0 row leaves, its fix said in the notes. Done 2026-10-23 (2218179a), the amend rule's row marked fixed on `main` too; serve's doubled line is left out until the M1's acceptance says whether it recurred.
- **The notes and the tag.** docs/releases/v0.5.0.md drafts the release notes, as v0.4.0's did, for the person to read before the tag (drafted 2026-10-23, its acceptance lines open until the M1's run ends); the tag goes on the accepted candidate's commit at the person's typed yes in the implementation thread; its GitHub Release is published from the notes at his typed "publish".
- **The port and the announcement.** The MacPorts port is updated to v0.5.0 through dockhand, as #35788 was, its pull request opened at the person's OK, and the mailing-list follow-up that waited for v0.5.0 (the person's "Update at 0.4.0", 2026-10-17 18:55Z) is drafted for him to post.

**Then v0.5.0, the final state** (two releases to it, the person's "Two releases", 2026-10-13; the order's history is in [the roadmap's history](activity/2026-10-16-roadmap-history.md#from-next-on-2026-10-17-v040s-parts-and-v050-as-it-stood)). After it, Then's items come at the person's word, and the release after v0.5.0 is v0.6.0, cut once Then's items 1 to 5 are done (the person's "After item 5", 2026-10-24 11:59Z, on the prioritization thread's card): it carries main's fixes placed above for the release after v0.5.0, the corpus's rest, and those five items, about 8 batches after v0.5.0's tag; items 6 to 15 follow it. A harm found in a release gets a point release on the quick stage alone, and field testing exercises main between tags.

2. **v0.5.0: layer 1** (about 33 batches, in six parts, all done 2026-10-22). One record of what dockhand knows about a branch, and one verdict on it, which status, tidy, submit, serve, and the hold list all read, where each judges a branch for itself today. Each step is replayed on terraform, py-lmdb, qemu, perl5, and the stub. It migrates the database, so the schema changes that waited for a release that migrates come here. The parts go in this order because each makes the next cheaper: the contracts and the assembly first, since everything after reads them; the record before the edits that write to it; the verdict on the record; the interface on the verdict; and new reach last, as the one part nothing depends on, which moves to Then if v0.5.0 runs long. Until 2026-10-17 its items were numbered 5.1 to 5.9; the old number is given with each.
   - **Part 1: contracts, assembly, and a failure you can work on** (about 12.5 batches). Each is independent of the ledger, and either fixes a gap a review traced, gives a rule one owner, is where later parts are wired, or makes a failed check one a person or an agent can work on without another full check.
     1. **The review's small fixes** (one batch; done 2026-10-17, [note](activity/2026-10-17-v050-small-fixes.md)). Each is a few lines and a test:
        - **A Cargo reading that found no program counts none.** `Reading.CargoProgram` (project/read.go) treats a nil `Programs` as every package a program, a fallback for readings made before `Programs` was kept, which `ReaderVersion` 7 no longer serves. `Programs` is nil, too, where a source has no src/main.rs or src/bin at all, as one whose programs are all `[[bin]]` with paths of their own, so the lib-only filter (sourcecompare/compare.go `cargoBinaries`) turns off and a library reads as a program: a binaries change that holds. Nil means none.
        - **A program added is a notice, not a hold.** A binaries change holds with "the Portfile's destroot may name the old ones" (assess.go `build`) when nothing was removed, as epic-lore's "lore-stamp added" did where the Portfile builds only lore-client and lore-server (Later's entry, from field testing's batch 15). One that only adds is said; one that removes or renames holds, as now.
        - **A guest that can't list its active ports doesn't report none.** The guest's `fact` returns "" for a failed command (buildenv/tart/guest.tcl), so a failed `port -q installed active` after a build is recorded as having read no other port, which reuse takes as complete inputs. A failure reports the inputs unknown, and the result isn't reused (`reuse.Current`).
        - **A renewed GitHub login is one login per process** (CX-11's first half). A renewal the Keychain didn't take is kept in the renewing client's memory alone (github/login.go `pendingLogin`); another client in the process, serve's identity watcher (command/serve.go `serveIdentity`) among them, reads the spent login, and serve stops acting on GitHub until `setup github`. By the code's reading, conditional on a save failing, not reproduced. The clients of one process share one pending login.
        - The three found after rc3, above, where they aren't in v0.4.0.
     2. **The source a port fetches, compared whole** (CX-2, half a batch; was the end of 5.1; done 2026-10-17, [note](activity/2026-10-17-v050-whole-source.md)). `ReleaseStateOf` and `SameSource` (macports/scope.go) leave out `git.url` and the fetch kind, so a change of repository alone, or from a tarball to git at the same version, reads as the source unchanged: `assessPort` says there's nothing upstream to compare, and the protected-sibling check can't see the change. Both join the compared state, an unknown value never counts as equal, and the assessment policy version is bumped, so stale assessments are collected again. Done when a `git.url`-only change and a fetch-kind change are each assessed, and `--follow-moves`' sibling protection still takes a legitimate move. Nothing migrates, since the state isn't stored.
     3. **One checked correction** (CX-3, one batch; was the end of step 3, moved here since step 3 then changes one operation, not three; done 2026-10-17, [note](activity/2026-10-17-v050-checked-correction.md)). Dockhand's corrections to its own edits are held to one fidelity check. Removing a patch upstream merged (portedit/patches.go) checks only the patch list, the version, and the revision before it replaces `Prepared`, where the Go toolchain and stealth-directory corrections compare the whole family against an explicit allowed change and record a report; so a setting that depends on the patch list, as `use_autoreconf` or a build dependency inside `if {[llength ${patchfiles}] > 0}`, or a sibling subport sharing the list, can change unsaid. One validated-correction operation in portedit owns the snapshot before, the candidate's evaluation, the whole-family comparison against the allowed change (for patch removal, the patch list alone), and publishing the files, snapshot, and report together; a correction can't replace `Prepared` without it. Patch removal moves onto it first, then the toolchain and stealth corrections. With the toolchain's move, CX-9's narrow part: `raiseGoToolchain` says it never refuses the bump, but a go.mod it can't read (a duplicate or irregular member, a forge error) fails the update in depblock's words (portedit/go_toolchain.go `goRequirement`); it's a notice that the minimum wasn't checked, as D4 holds an unattended one for a look.
     4. **One owner for each rule** (about 1.5 batches; the architecture review of 5897a82's second-owner batch, at the person's "Now", 2026-10-19 18:44Z; [note](activity/2026-10-19-architecture-review-coalesced.md); done 2026-10-19, [closing note](activity/2026-10-19-one-owner-closed.md)). Since 2026-10-15 each fix put its rule in the engine, but a call site's copy stayed, or a new copy was written beside it a day later, so four decisions have two owners and drift: the rc4 fix for `ship --mine` put the no-empty-branch rule at the command's call site, where `Engine.Ship` hard-codes it. Each line below deletes a copy and adds a test. R3-2 and R3-27's help text come first, in a commit of their own (done 2026-10-19, 2ec02f27; no rc6 carries them, the person's "No rc6"):
        - **The branch's active check is one predicate** (R3-2, the one that changes behaviour). `BranchStatus.Active` takes every queued or running run, where `ActiveCheck` and the queue drop baselines and runs asked to stop (engine/status.go, runner.go), so `CheckStanding` says Checking, and serve's `PassingBranches` waits, while a baseline of a passed check runs. All three read one predicate, with a test of the baseline.
        - **The drifted text** (R3-27): "queue" in the `--json` list, a command gone since d997488, and outdated's help naming `update --outdated --mine`. Each constructor marks `--json`, so the list goes, and the help-reference test reads literals that don't start with "dockhand ".
        - **The serve path's major hold, once** (R3-1): `goOn`'s goes (engine/ship.go) and `Update`'s stays, `Ship` saying its `HeldBeforeEdit`.
        - **ship chooses a port's branch through `Select`** (R3-3, R3-25). `shipTargets` re-implements the 0, 1, or many rule (command/ship.go) the day after `Select` took it, and author.go repeats two of its cases; it calls `Select` and `settleSelection`. `Select`'s `what` and `purpose` become typed, and `errStartForVersion` goes, so the new call isn't written against the strings (R3-25, which the review places now but its list leaves out).
        - **`NoHandBranch` decided in one place** (R3-11): `UpdateRequest` decides it from `Unattended` and a batch.
        - **submit's plan says whether a check stands for this ask** (R3-8): `PlanSubmit` takes the `Where` and the tests asked and sets `CheckNeeded` by `Covered`; the glue at command/submit.go goes, and `WhereRequest.Explicit()` with it.
        - **Who answered, read once** (R3-9's engine half, R3-36): `ArchiveBranch` asks `AppliesUnasked(AnsweredNobody, false)`, and serve sets `PrepareOptions.Answered`, where `prepareOne` infers it from the origin.
        - **One aside name** (R3-13): `RenameArchived` and `renameAside` choose `-archived`, `-2`, and on one way, inside one `store.Recorded`.
        - **ship's submission in ship.go** (R3-12): `prepareSubmit` and `submitPassed`, which only ship calls, move there, and ship.go and submit.go stop importing each other.
        - **Five duplicates below the engine, each to one composer:** the "older than this Mac's MacPorts" sentence (R3-7); the Tart 2.39.0 refusal, in `tart` (R3-18); the liveness window, in coord, which item 7's process list reads (R3-19); a release's name, `macos.Release`, with the code-organization review's finding 14 from Taken when touched (R3-29); and evaluator.tcl's `<build>` substitution (R3-26).
        - **planning without macos, and one profile vocabulary** (R3-15, R3-16): a typed exclusion reason, where five places read a sentence's prefix back, with the product name passed in; and one package owns the profile rules, the other reading them.
        - **The rest:** `newRoot(settings, streams)`, which `Run` and the help-reference test call, so the `inspectRoot` seam goes (R3-22); `check.tests`' default resolved in `ProposeCheck` (R3-35); `branchPort` reading the port from the selector (R3-37); one reader of `RefreshPullRequests` (R3-39's second half); the exports nothing outside their package uses, unexported, and `Engine.Held` as `SubmitPlan.Held()` (R3-28, R3-10's signature); one launchd plist producer, in macos (R3-21's second half, but for provision's agent plist, which Taken when touched places); and docs/architecture.md brought up to date (R3-33).
        - It closes as Maintenance's relocations say: its note lists the call sites switched, and a grep for each old rule's shape finds none left.
     5. **Why a check failed, from its log** (about 1 batch; the exiv2 field notes, 2026-10-19, [note](activity/2026-10-19-exiv2-field-notes.md); done 2026-10-19, [note](activity/2026-10-19-why-tests-failed.md), with [the baseline](activity/2026-10-19-new-tests-no-baseline.md) and [the attempts](activity/2026-10-19-logs-say-attempts.md)). It touches neither X1's seams nor the record, so it goes before v0.4.0's tag, after item 4, while X1 waits for the tag.
        - **A failed test's own output comes back.** After a test phase fails, the guest appends a capped tail of the test logs MacPorts' build systems leave in `test.dir` (CMake's Testing/Temporary/LastTest.log, automake's test-suite.log, meson's meson-logs/testlog.txt) to the target's log, under a `dockhand:` marker; today only the target's log and archives come back, and the VM goes with the rest (buildenv/tart/provider.go). The summary names the failed tests, from ctest's "The following tests FAILED:" lines, for a result whose tests failed under `declared` too, where nothing reads the log today (engine/runner.go). exiv2's ended at `make: *** [test] Error 8` and a pointer to LastTest.log, so the Portfile was changed to run `ctest --output-on-failure` just to see why.
        - **A dependency install is quiet where nothing builds from source.** The guest installs dependencies with `-d` (guest.tcl), so tar's `x ./opt/local/…` listing and "activating file:" lines made most of exiv2's 74,000-line log. Without `-d` where `fromsource` is empty; with it where something builds, since D10's compiler-error reading and MacPorts' "Command failed:" line need it there.
        - **`logs` says each environment's attempts.** A port's view starts with the environment, "attempt N of 3", the run, and the log's path, `.gz` once compressed, with what ended an earlier attempt (queue.go skips a result with no log, and names the environment only where there are several). A failed check's report lists each environment's log paths and any retry's reason, where it says only `dockhand logs check-N` (command/check.go). An interrupted attempt's partial log is fetched where the guest still answers, else its empty directory goes: exiv2's check-448 had an empty tart-macos27-arm64-1/ beside -2/, and nothing said why.
        - **`--baseline` isn't offered for tests master doesn't run.** `BaselineCandidates` (engine/baseline.go) counts any test failure, and asks only that master has the Portfile; a port whose only failures are tests master doesn't declare, by the change record's `dockhand.test_run`, is said instead ("exiv2's tests are new on this branch; master declares none"), and `check.baseline` doesn't run its baseline.
     6. **X1, `compose`, and `term`** (about 3 batches; was 5.8, moved ahead of the record and the split; done 2026-10-21, [note](activity/2026-10-21-x1-path-outside.md): command's 214 tests run in parallel, each on its own environment, nothing below compose reads the process's environment past the boundary test's shrinking list, and `term` is cut). Command fills one `engine.Dependencies` and a host value, in place of its package-global seams: the engine's test seams (settings.go, author.go, create.go) and about 20 host seams (auth.go, serve.go, providers.go, cleanup_*.go, watch.go; the four `= macos.X` vars in serve.go and auth.go among them, R3-21's first half), with X3's engine half and A-H9's fixtures; together they free `t.Parallel` in command. `compose` is where they're filled. It's first because the split (part 4) would otherwise move verb packages with their seams still package-global, exporting each to a shared package to be deleted here, and because the record's explicit capabilities (part 2) are filled in `compose` once. With it:
        - **The concrete port reader leaves engine** (CX-5's package half): `evaluatedPorts` (engine/ports.go) and its archive planning and fetching (engine/archivediff.go), with the neutral types they return (`Dependent`, `FetchedArchive`, `ErrNoArchives`, `ErrNoPort`), into a MacPorts source-query package below engine, since `compose` builds the default reader outside it; distfetch's `OwnArchives` goes with its one caller, dropping distfetch's import of depblock.
        - **One GitHub runtime per process** (CX-11): forge, discovery, the source archiver (built anew for each assessment today, assessment.go), project inspection, Actions, and status's login line share one client, its login, and its rate-limit observations, with account changes cleared in one place rather than `dropForge`'s. The forge's archive bodies use that client's HTTP (forge/github/archive.go), as Actions' logs do. It replaces the process-global pending login (github/login.go, R3-32), and the spend log's environment half, `DOCKHAND_GITHUB_LOG`, its file, and the command line, moves to `compose`, github keeping `Spend` (R3-17). Later's conditional requests then have one place to live. With it, from Later, two of field testing's (2026-10-19, at 6dd7745c): a look whose estimate passes what's left of the hour's allowance, which the runtime knows from GitHub's answers, asks first on a terminal and is refused without one unless a flag says to go on, since other sessions share the login (`outdated` over ryandesign's 2,133 ports and subports said ~5,500 calls of 5,000 and went on, 106 looked up after ten minutes); and subports that share one source, as the php and p5 ones that made most of that list, are looked up once.
        - **A command that names a branch finds its checkout from outside the ports tree** (the exiv2 field notes; here, not in item 5, since the checkout is chosen where `compose` will choose it, settings.go's `options`). `dockhand path <branch>` run elsewhere fails "not a MacPorts ports tree", though the branch's worktree is recorded, since the tree is opened before the database (engine.go `Open`). Where no tree is given and the directory isn't one, a command naming a branch takes the one registered checkout whose Git directory still exists, read by a new `Store.Repositories`; with several, it refuses, naming them. `path` with no branch still means here.
        - From the command package split: `engine.Setup`'s move and `offerSetup` (7b rows 3 and 4); the device flow to `github.Login`/`Logout`, with the `authFlow`/`authStore`/`authAPI` seams (7a row 3); one `Invocation` and the environment catalogue, 2c's `config.Environment` (7d rows 1 and 10); and `term` cut.
     7. **A check you can work on, failed or passed** (about 3 batches, with following a log; done 2026-10-22: [the pipeline once](activity/2026-10-22-check-pipeline-once.md), [check --keep and shell](activity/2026-10-22-check-keep.md), [check -d's drain](activity/2026-10-22-check-d-drain.md), and [status's processes](activity/2026-10-22-status-processes.md); after X1, so the new verb and the spawner are written against `compose`, but for following a log, which goes first, before X1 (2026-10-21); the exiv2 field notes, Later's `check --keep` from the Vx port's, and the architecture review of 5897a82's R3-4 and R3-14).
        - **First, the check pipeline once** (R3-4, R3-14; moved from part 4, item 1, since this item is the next to add to it). `Where`, `ProposeCheck`, `Covered`, `Runnable`, `Enqueue`, and a policy for a run already active are written three times, check's (command/check.go), ship's `queueHead`, and `Engine.Ship`'s, and the policy three ways: drive or follow decided by `Leader == nil` and passed to `SeeThrough(drive)`, `replaceActive`'s refuse or replace, and ship's follow where `PlanCovers`. v0.4.0's part 2, item 4 said the pipeline "written three times becomes one", and is marked done; three copies are left. One `engine.QueueCheck` carries the covered and active-run decisions, `Enqueue` returns the run it followed and takes `Replace`, and `SeeThrough` decides drive or follow from the lead itself; check and ship render its result. The kept guest's `Plan.Keep` and the drain below are then each written once, in the engine's pipeline: the drain is `SeeThrough`'s case where nobody leads, which `runQueued` says today as "Start dockhand serve to run it."
        - **A kept guest, and `shell`.** `check --keep` keeps each environment's clone, stopped, where its guest finished, whether its targets failed or passed (the person's "Add that as well.", 2026-10-20 03:20Z, to keeping one that passed), so a shell can look at what a passing build installed as well as at a failure; never on infrastructure trouble, where the guest didn't finish (`Plan.Keep`, in the plan's JSON beside `Fresh`). Only a check a person runs keeps one, never serve's or ship's, and a kept clone is never a base another check builds on. A stopped clone holds no VM slot, only disk; automatic cleanup spares it for 24 hours unless space is low, and `clean` and the branch's next check remove it. `dockhand shell check-N [--on <release>] [-- <command>]` starts it, runs the command in the port's work directory, streams its output, exits with its code, and stops it; with no command, on a terminal, it opens a shell. The command form is the one an agent can use. `shell check-N --test` copies the branch's port directory as it is now to the path the guest staged it at, and reruns the test phase alone with `port -o`, which keeps the build though the Portfile changed; an edit outside the test phase doesn't apply, which it says, and the run is never a result or evidence. exiv2 took four full checks, 8 to 10 minutes each, to find a dyld rpath abort through `pre-test { system "otool …" }` blocks.
        - **A long check without a terminal** (with the gnome-voice session's, which needed `--replace` to run its check here once `-d` had queued it). `check -d` with no serve leading starts `serve --drain` in its own session, notifying nobody and logging beside the database, as automatic cleanup is started (command/cleanup_unix.go), and says so: "a background drain (pid N) runs it; dockhand wait check-N follows it". A drain that found the queue empty reads it once more after letting go of the lead, so a check queued meanwhile isn't left. An agent's foreground check outlives its tool's timeout today, and field testing detaches checks with nohup since a session's end killed one.
        - **Following a running target's log** (the gnome-voice session's notes). The driver copies a target's log to the host as the guest writes it, at each read of the guest's results, where today it fetches the log when the target ends (buildenv/tart/provider.go `follow`, `partialLog`), so `logs check-N <port> --follow` prints it as it grows, in any process, and stops when the target ends; a check on GitHub says it can't be followed and names the run's page. Done when a running target's log follows, and the README's known issue on `logs` of a running check goes. On main 2026-10-21 (a79de414), tested with a scripted guest and a copied log; the known issue is gone; field testing's next running check follows it.
        - **status lists the dockhand processes running.** Each live session by coord's one liveness window (item 4; coord's `sessions`: kind, pid, build, start, heartbeat), with what it holds, the lead or a check, and whether the schema it expects is the database's, from a `schema` column each session records (a migration). The exiv2 agent compared `pgrep` and versions by hand before each rebuild, as field testing does. Commands that open no session, such as submit and update, stay unlisted.
     8. **Forgetting an archived branch** (one batch; was 5.9, moved earlier, as nothing depends on it and v0.5.0 migrates anyway; done 2026-10-22, [note](activity/2026-10-22-forget-branch.md), with a forgotten mark, schema 34, over keeping the reused executions alone). `clean --delete` deletes an archived branch's Git branch and its record, asking first, saying what it deletes, unmerged commits included, and needing `--yes` without a terminal; it reads `CleanStep.Kind` and SQL-S2's foreign-key-ordered delete. Its record can't simply go: other branches' results name its executions in `results.reused_from`, a text column, not a foreign key, so it needs the reused executions kept, or a forgotten state, a schema change. With it, Later's "status says what clean kept": clean records what it kept of a cleaned branch, as "fork branch kept: moved after merge", and `status --all` says it.
   - **Part 2: one record** (about 5.5 batches).
     1. **One revision analysis (C1)** (about 3 batches; was 5.1; done 2026-10-22, [note](activity/2026-10-22-revision-analysis.md); `movedSources` and status's source-moved check stay where they are, since they read the network, and the single-file distfile was one judgment already), in a new engine/revision.go keyed by tree and base, which the change record, the assessments, and the plan read instead of each deriving its own (Codex's review of 386ac2cc, finding 4).
        - **C3 first: every capability explicit** (with CX-5). The port reader's capabilities are found by type assertion at seven sites: `FamilyReader` (changes.go), `dependencyGraphReader` (plan.go), `ArchivePlanner` (assessment.go), `ArchiveFetcher` (archivediff.go), `portNamer`, asserted on the dependent reader (diff.go), `DependentReader` (diff.go), and `MaintainerReader` (maintainer.go); and the forge's at `renamed()` (update.go), which turns rename detection off. Most missing ones do something silently: no change records, direct dependencies alone, nothing collected, subports unnamed, no rename found. Each becomes a field `compose` fills, the narrow interfaces kept as parameter types, and the three override fields that shadow assertions today (`ArchivePlanner`, `ArchiveFetcher`, `DependentReader`) go; a missing capability is said or is an error. The one production reader has every capability, so what changes is the tests, which today run most of engine with change records and graph ordering off. `throughUnchanged`'s traversal moves to `planning`, beside `Needs` and `Narrow`.
        - **The record says what it was evaluated under** (CX-6). A change record keeps the evaluator's runtime (MacPorts Base, Tcl, the platform modelled), which every snapshot carries already, as provenance: its JSON is kept whole, so nothing migrates. Records are made natively today and the plan doesn't read them; where this item makes the plan read them, a record from another runtime is read by `UnseenChanges`' rule, as a platform's is. Assessments keep their key until a failure is seen that needs the runtime in it.
        - L2a, an archive the new version no longer fetches, said and held (a README known issue), and L2's one-producer half: a branch's first check makes its change record through the same producer as everything else.
        - It absorbs the local "what changed" logic, `movedSources`, status's source-moved check, and the single-file distfile; and moves update.go's branch selection to branches.go, the useful part of M1, with `Update`'s seams (code-organization finding 44). The semantic predicates `SourceUnchanged` and `NothingCompared` leave words.go for the analysis, which produces them, and `ComparisonLines`, whose `Hold` is a judgment, with them (R3-23).

   6. **Status gathered once** (C8-6, about 1.5 batches with its speed; here, by its own last sentence, since item 7 quotes a CI line in status): one `engine.StatusReport`, each branch's judged status, its link findings, and its attention rows, gathered before anything renders, and the text and JSON rendered from it alone. Today `ShowStatus` judges each branch twice under `--json`, asks the engine for `UndeclaredLinks` inside its rendering loop, and view's `WriteNextFor` opens and judges a branch itself; status makes thirteen engine calls spread through its rendering. The gathering is where `status --all`'s time goes too (the M1's dhtest measure on rc1, 2026-10-25: 84 s for 683 branches, rc5's 68 s; the fix for main above takes the remotes): each branch's head, tree, and commit count is read for all branches at once, one `git for-each-ref` and one `git cat-file --batch`, where each branch runs its own three today (`readFiles`); and an ended branch, merged, closed, or archived, skips its holds, its hand edits, and whether it landed, which say nothing of a branch nothing is submitted from. Its files are still read: an archived branch's Git branch can still move, and its line names its commits and ports, which the coordinator's "skip git entirely for archived branches whose worktrees are gone" would read from records that may be behind. rc1's 16 s over rc5 is what items 3 and 4 added to each branch (inferred), and is measured before and after.    7. **After submit: a follow-up branch, and CI's error line** (about 1.5 batches together; the reflections' F, with the exiv2 session's per-port CI, 2026-10-24). A branch whose pull request merged with commits on it beyond the merge gets a Next, `dockhand start --from <branch>`, which starts a new branch from master and picks those commits onto it, branchstate's verdict and one engine operation (wasmedge's follow-up was made by hand); `start --from` over architecture's `tidy --carry`, since starting a branch is start's. And an unclassified CI failure's status quotes the job log's first error line, read as a local log's is (D10), where today it says the log said nothing of its cause (xapian-core). And a pull request's CI run is read a port at a time: MacPorts' CI builds each port in turn in one job, so status says each port's result, "CI: exiv2 ✓, gpsbabel ✗ (build)", from the log it reads for the error line (the exiv2 session's notes, verified, which summarized it by hand).

   8. **The patch refresh** (about 1.5 batches; the reflections' B). patchcheck grows Refresh: a patch that applies with fuzz or offset is applied, re-emitted from the applied tree with its header comment kept, and the hunks that landed with fuzz or in a generated file are said, the generated file by a path rule. portedit applies it as a correction, as `dropMergedPatches` is applied, with an exact prediction: the tree after the regenerated patch applies cleanly equals the tree after the old one applied with fuzz. The regenerated patch is in the pull request's diff for review.

   11. **The survey rules as counterfactual notices on the ledger** (about 2.25 batches): three of the four the person kept on 2026-10-02, lines a PortGroup already sets, path-style dependencies, and offering a `Closes:` trailer for a Trac ticket, the revbump classifier being item 9's; each a notice that tests its claim by evaluating the alternative, on lines the diff touched alone, holding nothing until its false positives are measured. - **serve doesn't propose again an update closed unmerged** (about a quarter batch; the dig's I1, from v1's lifecycle and v2's queue, checked against main's 1186127b). `PlanOutdated` (engine/outdated.go) leaves alone only a port an open branch changes (`BranchesChanging` reads open branches alone). When a pull request is closed unmerged, the follower closes its branch (follow.go), so the next day's look plans the same version again, under a new name, since the closed branch keeps its own (`NameFor`). Under `serve.ship_to = "submit"`, serve checks it and opens a pull request again: the search for other pull requests reads open ones, and the closed one's head has another name (`Earlier`), so the next day undoes a maintainer's close, and each close after it, up to `submit_limit` a day; under `commit` or `check`, the branch is made again once (read in the code, not run). Probably as old as `ship_to` (v0.4.0), so not a regression. `PlanOutdated` also leaves alone a port whose closed or archived branch updated it to the version it would take, saying so where it says its other skips ("jq: 1.8.1's pull request #123 was closed unmerged, in jq-1.8.1; a newer release lifts this"), and a newer version lifts it. An attended `update` or `bump` of it goes ahead, saying the earlier close. Done when an engine test's outdated pass skips a port whose branch closed at the version it would take, plans it at a newer one, and serve's submit pass opens no pull request for it.

   8. **The patch refresh** (about 1.75 batches; the reflections' B). patchcheck grows Refresh: a patch that applies with fuzz or offset is applied, re-emitted from the applied tree with its header comment kept, and the hunks that landed with fuzz or in a generated file are said, the generated file by a path rule. portedit applies it as a correction, as `dropMergedPatches` is applied, with an exact prediction: the tree after the regenerated patch applies cleanly equals the tree after the old one applied with fuzz. The regenerated patch is in the pull request's diff for review. First, v1's D12 bounds it (the docs dig's I4, about a quarter batch; the person's ruling, "fuzz is never accepted unattended"; checked against 1186127b): patchcheck's `problems` matches failures alone, so a patch that applies only with fuzz passes an unattended run today, where a fuzzed hunk can land in the wrong place and still build. patchcheck tells fuzz from offset; a patch that applies only with fuzz holds an unattended run (rule `patch-fuzz`, in `HoldRules`, so `allow` can let it go) and is said on an attended one. The refresh then relocates a hunk unattended only where it applies verbatim at an offset and its before-block occurs once in the file; a fuzzed hunk's re-emitted patch is offered on an attended run alone, and stays held on serve's.

   9. **A library's install names, and its dependents** (about 1.5 batches; the reflections' E, with the survey rules' revbump classifier, which it is). The guest reads what the target's own dylibs are called, their LC_ID_DYLIB install names and compatibility versions (`otool -l`), a per-target observation beside links. The old version's come from the port's previous result in the same environment, where dockhand checked it, else from master's binary archive, installed and read before the build as dependency isolation installs archives, so it needs no baseline. evidence compares, purely, and a change is a notice naming the dependents and offering `--revbump-dependents`, and the version it compared against where that's older than master's; with no earlier reading it says nothing, unknown and never equal: exiv2 0.28's soname and its 38 dependents, raylib's SOVERSION, and brotli's, which was inferred rather than read.

   8. **The patch refresh** (about 2.25 batches; the reflections' B). patchcheck grows Refresh: a patch that applies with fuzz or offset is applied, re-emitted from the applied tree with its header comment kept, and the hunks that landed with fuzz or in a generated file are said, the generated file by a path rule. portedit applies it as a correction, as `dropMergedPatches` is applied, with an exact prediction: the tree after the regenerated patch applies cleanly equals the tree after the old one applied with fuzz. Its I13 joins Then's item 11:

- **A check on GitHub refuses at once where the fork's workflow won't run** (about a quarter batch; the dig's I24, from v2's provider, which read `main.yml`'s state (docs/v2/github-verification.md); checked against 6cae7922). GitHub turns Actions off on a new fork (docs/github-provider.md), and the provider pushes `dockhand-check/<sha>`, then waits ten minutes for a run before saying "GitHub did not start … check that Actions are enabled for your fork" (buildenv/ghactions/provider.go), an infrastructure error, so a `retry` or serve's next pass spends ten minutes more; setup's github line can't say whether the fork's Actions will run (command/setup/providers.go). Before it pushes, the provider reads the fork's workflow, one request (`GET /repos/{fork}/actions/workflows/main.yml`); where it is missing or its state isn't active, it refuses at once, an environment refusal, as a missing Tart image is, naming the state and the fork's Actions page, and setup's github line reads the same. The wait stays for a run that doesn't start for any other reason. Done when a provider test's fork whose workflow is disabled is refused before any push, with its Actions URL, an active one pushes as today, and setup's line says the state.

- **An incomplete assessment is made again by a newer dockhand** (about a quarter batch; the dig's I26, from batch 52's note on pomo, "a recognizer fixed without a policy raise leaves the old refusal standing until the tree moves"; checked against 6cae7922). A revision's assessment is reused while its tree and base stand, incomplete ones too: `lookAgain` (engine/assessment.go) makes again only a transient problem, and an `UpstreamMissing` one after a day, so one of kind `Unsupported` or `Failed` stands until a file changes or `assess.Policy` is raised. Most of what field testing fixes turns such a refusal into a reading, and raising `Policy` for it is a habit (assess.go's comments), not a rule, so after an upgrade an open branch can keep its old "couldn't compare" hold. The assessment records the build that made it (buildinfo's version, one field stored with it), and `lookAgain` also makes again an incomplete one another build made, rather than a Maintenance rule to raise `Policy`, the habit pomo's fix missed. A record without the field reads as another build, so none is rewritten, and v0.7.0's first look makes again each incomplete one v0.6.0 made, so waiting for v0.7.0 loses nothing. A complete one stands across builds, `Policy` saying when its rules change. A development build differs at each commit, so field testing's builds make incomplete ones again, a few fetches each. Done when an engine test's incomplete `Unsupported` assessment is reused under the same build and made again under another, and a complete one stands under another.

From the M1's acceptance of rc4, its B9 (the Prime-time session's note, sent here by the coordinator on 2026-10-26 at 18:50Z), polish, not harm, so a fix for main riding v0.7.0 with the fixes above, and a known issue in v0.6.0's release notes:

- **A job GitHub cancelled before a runner took it says so** (about a tenth of a batch; checked against rc5's 4f87f012). When GitHub cancels a run's job before any runner picks it up, as it did while its macOS runners were down during B9, the provider still asks for the job's log, which doesn't exist, and the check's reason reads "reading macos-15's log: … job log: fetch: HTTP 404" (buildenv/ghactions/provider.go's `read`, github.go's `JobLog`). A job no runner took (`RunnerJob.RunnerName` empty, as `Release` already reads it) has no log, so the provider asks for none: the other jobs' results are read as before, and where nothing built, the reason says "GitHub cancelled macos-15's job before any runner took it; see <the run's URL>", still an infrastructure error, which `retry` meets again once GitHub's runners work. Done when a provider test's run whose macos-15 job was cancelled with no runner fetches no log for it and records the other jobs' results, and, with that job alone, gives that reason.

## From Next and the decisions on 2026-10-27: one policy for every selection, and ship off a terminal

The person ruled on the docs consolidation thread's re-review of standing decisions (2026-10-27 01:56Z and 02:02Z, sent here by the coordinator at 02:03Z). dockhand applies one policy to every selection, his own ports or one he names, so the split the selectors entry and its decision drew, where serve stopped another's port at its check, is withdrawn, and principles.md's "Submit unattended beyond the person's own ports" is struck, since MacPorts' review is the guard on a pull request for another's port. And ship off a terminal acts as `--yes`, as bump does, which settles run 4's "fine for now". Nothing here rides rc6. They stood:

- **Port selectors, and serve's selection** (about three quarters of a batch; the dig's I38a, from v2's CLI design, at the person's word above; Decided). `outdated`, `update`, and `ship` take named ports or `--mine` alone (command/outdated, author, ship), though `portindex.Filter`, which the survey tool alone uses, reads master's index by maintainer, `openmaintainer` and `nomaintainer` among them, by category, and by "not maintained by". None is harm: syft's refusal is clean and writes nothing, but it stops `update`, `bump`, and serve for every port whose Portfile reads `source_date_epoch` as it's parsed, syft, cloudflared, ctop, and socket_vmnet among them:

- **A modelled context reads `source_date_epoch`** (about a quarter batch; implementation's first option, narrowed, since a snapshot has no history to read). syft's Portfile passes `-X main.buildDate=${source_date_epoch}` (its line 33), and that option's default, `portmain::get_source_date_epoch` (portmain.tcl 169-248, alike in 2.12.6 and 2.12.7), runs `git -c safe.directory=* -C <dir> rev-parse --is-inside-work-tree` on the port's directory and its PortGroups', then `git status --porcelain` and `git log -1 --pretty=%ct` only where all are in a work tree, and otherwise takes the newest modification time of the port's files. The observation refuses any process in a modelled context (observation.tcl's `host_event`), so the version probe says "modeled context depends on host state: a host process executed while evaluating the Portfile". A tree dockhand evaluates is a snapshot materialized under the temporary directory with no `.git` (workspace.Open), so git, run in the Mac's own context, finds no work tree there, and base takes the modification times, which the observation admits as reads in the captured tree. In a modelled context the dispatcher answers that one probe, `rev-parse --is-inside-work-tree` on a directory in the captured tree, as git does, not a work tree, without a process, and every other process is still refused; the value is the one the Mac's own context gets, and the fingerprint already keeps it unstable (evaluator.tcl's `code_fingerprint`). Done when an evaluator test of a Portfile that reads `${source_date_epoch}` as it's parsed observes a modelled context with no host event and the snapshot's time, another `exec` in one still refuses, and `update syft --plan` on master's syft plans where rc5 refused.

From macports-dev's second outside report (Link Dupont, macports-dev 046989, 2026-10-27, reproduced by implementation on main and rc5 and seen by field testing on v0.5.0, so no regression, sent here by the coordinator at 01:46Z), and three fixes v0.7.0 led with (the person's "Add Syft + B9 + I24 + I26 + the Tart VM limit to rc6", 2026-10-27 01:52Z, in the prioritization thread, and his "Move to v0.7.0" for Tart's VMs, 01:55Z, on its card, since they'd have rerun S2 and B7), fixes for main before v0.6.0's tag, as rc6, at the person's choice of rc6 over 0.6.1 and v0.7.0 (2026-10-27 01:48Z, on the announcement thread's card). None is harm: syft's refusal is clean and writes nothing, but it stops `update`, `bump`, and serve for every port whose Portfile reads `source_date_epoch` as it's parsed, syft, uv, and cloudflared among them:

- **A check on GitHub refuses at once where the fork's workflow won't run** (about a quarter batch; the dig's I24, from v2's provider, which read `main.yml`'s state (docs/v2/github-verification.md); checked against 6cae7922). GitHub turns Actions off on a new fork (docs/github-provider.md), and the provider pushes `dockhand-check/<sha>`, then waits ten minutes for a run before saying "GitHub did not start … check that Actions are enabled for your fork" (buildenv/ghactions/provider.go), an infrastructure error, so a `retry` or serve's next pass spends ten minutes more; setup's github line can't say whether the fork's Actions will run (command/setup/providers.go). Before it pushes, the provider reads the fork's workflow, one request (`GET /repos/{fork}/actions/workflows/main.yml`); where it is missing or its state isn't active, it refuses at once, an environment refusal, as a missing Tart image is, naming the state and the fork's Actions page, and setup's github line reads the same. The wait stays for a run that doesn't start for any other reason. Done when a provider test's fork whose workflow is disabled is refused before any push, with its Actions URL, an active one pushes as today, and setup's line says the state.

- **An incomplete assessment is made again by a newer dockhand** (about a quarter batch; the dig's I26, from batch 52's note on pomo, "a recognizer fixed without a policy raise leaves the old refusal standing until the tree moves"; checked against 6cae7922). A revision's assessment is reused while its tree and base stand, incomplete ones too: `lookAgain` (engine/assessment.go) makes again only a transient problem, and an `UpstreamMissing` one after a day, so one of kind `Unsupported` or `Failed` stands until a file changes or `assess.Policy` is raised. Most of what field testing fixes turns such a refusal into a reading, and raising `Policy` for it is a habit (assess.go's comments), not a rule, so after an upgrade an open branch can keep its old "couldn't compare" hold. The assessment records the build that made it (buildinfo's version, one field stored with it), and `lookAgain` also makes again an incomplete one another build made, rather than a Maintenance rule to raise `Policy`, the habit pomo's fix missed. A record without the field reads as another build, so none is rewritten, and v0.6.0's first look makes again each incomplete one an earlier build made, as A7 from rc5 to rc6 sees. A complete one stands across builds, `Policy` saying when its rules change. A development build differs at each commit, so field testing's builds make incomplete ones again, a few fetches each. Done when an engine test's incomplete `Unsupported` assessment is reused under the same build and made again under another, and a complete one stands under another.

- **A job GitHub cancelled before a runner took it says so** (about a tenth of a batch; from the M1's acceptance of rc4, its B9, sent here by the coordinator on 2026-10-26 at 18:50Z, and a known issue in v0.6.0's release notes no longer; checked against rc5's 4f87f012). When GitHub cancels a run's job before any runner picks it up, as it did while its macOS runners were down during B9, the provider still asks for the job's log, which doesn't exist, and the check's reason reads "reading macos-15's log: … job log: fetch: HTTP 404" (buildenv/ghactions/provider.go's `read`, github.go's `JobLog`). A job no runner took (`RunnerJob.RunnerName` empty, as `Release` already reads it) has no log, so the provider asks for none: the other jobs' results are read as before, and where nothing built, the reason says "GitHub cancelled macos-15's job before any runner took it; see <the run's URL>", still an infrastructure error, which `retry` meets again once GitHub's runners work. Done when a provider test's run whose macos-15 job was cancelled with no runner fetches no log for it and records the other jobs' results, and, with that job alone, gives that reason.

**Then v0.6.0** (the person's "After item 5", 2026-10-24 11:59Z, on the prioritization thread's card): Then's items 1 to 5, the corpus's rest, main's fixes above, the `local` provider (the person's "I'm curious if we can get this into 0.6.0.", 2026-10-25 22:24Z), the docs dig's I1, and rc6's syft fix with the three it took from v0.7.0's lead, B9, I24, and I26, about 9.25 batches after v0.5.0's tag; Then's items 6 to 15 follow it as one release, **v0.7.0**, about 31 batches with the fixes and readings below and the docs dig's additions to items 7, 8, 9, 11, 14, and 15, with one acceptance, whose full stage builds one older Tart release too, so `minmax` runs as two releases, with two VMs and the wait for them (the docs dig's I29), as v0.5.0's 33 had (the person's "One release", 2026-10-26 06:22Z, on the prioritization thread's card, over two releases split after item 10). Field testing exercises main between tags.
