- **git's ssh never asks under serve** (about a tenth of a batch; the dig's I46, from acceptance's D-C6, which never ran its passphrase path). `GIT_TERMINAL_PROMPT=0` (git/repository.go) keeps git's HTTPS from asking, and nothing does for its ssh, so a push over ssh with a passphrase key and no agent may ask for it under serve, where no one can answer. - **Two small corrections** (about a tenth of a batch; the dig's I39 and I44's middle part). The advice to copy the database, "copy the database and point DOCKHAND_DB at the copy" (engine/engine.go) and "Restore it from a backup" (store/sqlite/sqlite.go), says `sqlite3 <database> ".backup <file>"`, since a plain copy of a WAL database dockhand has open can miss its last writes. And each optional provider capability gets its compile-time assertion, `var _ buildenv.<Capability> = (*Provider)(nil)`, as the script and github providers have some (script.go, ghactions/provider.go), so a signature slip can't quietly turn one off. Done when the build fails for a provider whose capability's signature drifts.

Base's master reads `extract.only`, and so `distfiles`, as it opens a port (portextract.tcl's `find_methods`, a callback `mportopen` runs after the Portfile), so a Portfile whose `distfiles` errors can't be opened there at all, as `port info` would say, and dockhand's failed evaluation of it is MacPorts' answer. `TestAFailedMetadataProbeIsRecorded` makes its probe fail through what only the probe reads, `use_configure` with no `distfiles`, so it tests the probe on 2.12 and master alike. Done when the test passes on both.

Its wait for the stopped check to have changed the prefix, libharbor in the registry (buildenv/local/local_test.go's `require.Eventually`), is bounded at 10 seconds, while that check starts MacPorts' tclsh, runs the guest program, takes the prefix's snapshot, and installs a port, beside every other package's tests; the guest's own waits for a step are 30 seconds (guest_test.go's `TestTheGuestSaysWhatItIsBuilding`), and the command package's are `cmdtest.Settle`, two minutes, since five seconds ran out on CI's runner (cmdtest/world.go, 8d2cb046). The note doesn't say which wait timed out, so the bound is the inferred cause, not one seen; CI runs this test on its macOS runners too (.github/scripts/macports.sh sets DOCKHAND_TEST_MACPORTS_TCLSH), so it can turn a push red there. The settle moves to testsupport, cmdtest's naming it, and each `Eventually` elsewhere that waits on a process or another goroutine takes it in place of its seconds: local's, the guest's two, script's two, coord's two, the runner's, the PortIndex cache's, https's, progress's, and provision's. It is a bound, not a pace: each wait still ends as soon as its condition holds, and one that asserts something happens within a time keeps its bound and says so. From the weekly job against MacPorts base's master (the docs dig's I43, below), its first findings, at base's 22f546e2 of 2026-10-23 (implementation's note, sent here by the coordinator on 2026-10-26 at 23:09Z), two fixes for main before base's next release. Neither is harm, since v0.6.0 and the Macs it runs on read Portfiles with released Base 2.12; should base release before v0.7.0 does, the second is weighed for a point release then. The job stays red until the second lands, which would hide a new difference, so the evaluator's fix goes first after v0.6.0's tag, after the tests' settle above, ahead of the fixes below:

- **A modelled context's SDK and tools are its own where Base asks from its parent interpreter** (about a batch). Base's master moves its SDK and compiler lookups out of the port's interpreter into its parent's, `portlib::configure` (`get_sdkroot`, `find_close_sdk`, `file_exists`, `find_developer_tool`, `get_system_compiler_version`, aliased into each worker in macports.tcl), where dockhand's dispatcher (toolchain.tcl) doesn't answer: their `file exists`, `glob`, and `xcrun` see the Mac, so a modelled macOS 12 context reads the Mac's CommandLineTools/SDKs/MacOSX.sdk where the table gives MacOSX12.sdk (`TestAModelledContextTakesItsToolsFromTheTable`), and Base caches those answers for the process (`file_exists_cache`, `sdkroot_cache`). The evaluator's preview adapter already says master asks questions dockhand doesn't observe (eval/compatibility.go), and a released Base 2.13 would be admitted with a warning alone (`admitBase`). The modelled toolchain answers Base's questions wherever Base asks them, in the worker or its parent, each context from its own row of the table, and Base's caches never carry one context's answer into another's. Done when the weekly job's evaluator tests pass against base's master, that test among them, and ci.yml's against 2.12 pass unchanged.

From the same dig and word, and from field testing's note on v0.5.0 (sent here by the coordinator on 2026-10-26 at 15:15Z), two fixes for main riding v0.7.0, after v0.6.0's tag and the evaluator's fix above, and ahead of Then's item 6, since each is correctness and small, though none is harm: the unattended road doesn't reach the dig's I3, and clean keeps rather than loses; its I6 rides rc6 (above). The dig's I2, I4, and I7 join Then's items 7, 8, and 11, and its I5 is declined (Decided):

- **An update to a lower version owes an epoch** (about half a batch; the dig's I3, from the v1 design's D27, "A downgrade owes an epoch increment" (the person, 2026-09-06), and design-v3 §8's commit-rules table, whose "Version order" rule was never built; checked against 1186127b). upstream/resolve.go refuses an explicit version only where it is the current one, so `update jq 1.6` on 1.7 writes a version `port upgrade` never takes, stranding every install, and nothing says so. Outdated holds a port ahead of every upstream version (`Ahead`), so serve doesn't reach it. The rule is base's own predicate, `macports::_plan_upgrade` (macports.tcl 5245-5252 at v2.12.6), read through the evaluator's `vercmp`, not a naive order: a version `vercmp` orders lower upgrades only where `epoch` rises too, and an epoch raised at an unchanged version does nothing. An update to a version base wouldn't upgrade to raises `epoch` by one and says so in its plan, writing the line where the target's own context sets it, else after `revision`, else after `version`, and guarding on the command, not its evaluated value (py-openssl sets it inside an `if`). commitrules gets the rule as an error, for a commit that lowers a port's version without raising its epoch, and for an obsoleting `replaced_by` with no version, revision, or epoch change. Done when update's test writes the epoch for a lower version and not for a scheme change `vercmp` orders forward, and commitrules' tests flag both shapes.

The engine reads those logs through buildenv/ghactions (`PortResults`, `Untimed`, `FirstError`), the first provider its boundary allows, where the engine takes `buildenv`'s contract alone and the command layer composes the providers. So next, ahead of item 8 (about a tenth of a batch), the job log's readers, logs.go with `ReadLog`, `Built`, and `ListsSubports`, which need model alone, move to a small macports package, `internal/macports/cilog`, beside macports' ci.go, which reads the same workflow's matrix; ghactions and the engine both read it, and the engine's boundary drops ghactions. Done when no provider is on the engine's import list and the readers' tests pass in their new package.
   8. **The patch refresh** (about 2.5 batches; the reflections' B). patchcheck grows Refresh: a patch that applies with fuzz or offset is applied, re-emitted from the applied tree with its header comment kept, and the hunks that landed with fuzz or in a generated file are said, the generated file by a path rule. portedit applies it as a correction, as `dropMergedPatches` is applied, with an exact prediction: the tree after the regenerated patch applies cleanly equals the tree after the old one applied with fuzz. The regenerated patch is in the pull request's diff for review. First, v1's D12 bounds it (the docs dig's I4, about a quarter batch; the person's ruling, "fuzz is never accepted unattended"; checked against 1186127b): patchcheck's `problems` matches failures alone, so a patch that applies only with fuzz passes an unattended run today, where a fuzzed hunk can land in the wrong place and still build. patchcheck tells fuzz from offset; a patch that applies only with fuzz holds an unattended run (rule `patch-fuzz`, in `HoldRules`, so `allow` can let it go) and is said on an attended one. The refresh then relocates a hunk unattended only where it applies verbatim at an offset and its before-block occurs once in the file; a fuzzed hunk's re-emitted patch is offered on an attended run alone, and stays held on serve's. And a hunk upstream already carries is dropped (the docs dig's I22, about a quarter batch, at the person's "Everything in that Tier 2 keep list looks good. Send them all.", 2026-10-26 20:24Z; field testing's reflections, confluent-cli's trailing-newline hunk and rsync's): where `patch` reports a hunk as previously applied and the rest of the patch applies, the refresh re-emits the patch without it, saying so in patchcheck's line, with the same exact prediction, the tree after the refreshed patch equals the tree after the old one with that hunk skipped; `dropMergedPatches` still drops a patch whose every hunk is applied. Last, what the refresh can't mend stops an unattended run before its check (the docs dig's I9, about a quarter batch, placed in Later on 2026-09-30 and dropped without a word; checked against f5af695b): an assessment's `patch-rejected` holds nothing today ("a patch that doesn't apply fails the build, which a check catches", assess/patches.go), so serve, `bump`, and `ship --yes` commit the update and spend a VM's dependency installs and extract on a check that fails at its patch phase, as field testing's e2fsprogs and net-snmp did (engine/ship.go queues the check whatever the assessment found). `patch-rejected` joins `HoldRules`, and an unattended ship stops before it proposes the check, the branch left at its committed edit, saying the patch, its failed hunks, and that `allow` lets it build; attended `ship` and `check` build as today, since patchcheck's reading isn't the build's. It comes after the refresh, so a patch the refresh mends, at an offset or with a hunk upstream carries, never stops serve. First of all, patchcheck reads git's quoted names (the docs dig's I30, about a tenth of a batch, at the person's word of 2026-10-26 22:20Z; the library survey's leftover): `targets()` skips a name that starts with a quote, git's form for spaces, tabs, and non-ASCII, and splits `diff --git` with `strings.Fields`, so such a patch's file isn't extracted and the patch reads as not applying, which `patch-rejected` would hold unattended, as said above, so this comes before that hold; it unquotes git's form with `strconv.Unquote` and takes the `---` and `+++` names where they're given. And the dozen portedit sites that take a change's `Files[0]` for the Portfile read each file by its path (the docs dig's I44, its first part, about a tenth of a batch), since the refresh is the first edit to write a patch file beside it.
